Abstract (英文): A live esports broadcast needs a director who chooses which camera goes on air and a caster who talks over that choice, every second and without knowing what comes next. Streaming video language models have learned to commentate, but only over a broadcast that a human director has already produced. We study live broadcast production, in which a model receives the raw camera of every player and must both direct and commentate from the past alone. We present DirCast, a single streaming video language model that does both. Every second it scans all cameras at low resolution, decides with a hold-or-switch director whether and where to cut, and reads the chosen camera at high resolution before it speaks. Its context keeps what went on air and what was said and lets the other views expire, which bounds the cost of a stream of any length. We build a dataset of 27 professional Dota 2 matches with a camera for every hero, the human director's choices and the casters' commentary. DirCast directs better than rule-based and zero-shot directors including GPT-5, and its commentary wins 29.2 percent of comparisons against GPT-4o under the Proact-VL protocol, against 19.4 percent for the strongest of eleven streaming commentators that watch the human broadcast.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing 3D talking avatar methods mainly rely on parametric face models including 3DMM or FLAME, whose compact motion representations limit their ability to synthesize extreme expressions and to model accessories such as headwear. We introduce AudioLift, a two-stage framework that uses generated multi-view videos as the intermediate representation for audio-driven avatar generation without parametric face models or per-instance optimization. Our framework combines a synchronized multi-view video generation stage with an inconsistency-robust Gaussian reconstruction stage to recover coherent full-head geometry from a single portrait image. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AudioLift produces 3D talking avatars capable of capturing more extreme facial expressions than existing methods, with improved geometric completeness and strong multi-view consistency across the full $360^\circ$ viewing range.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generating multiple videos of a dynamic scene from a single reference video requires synthesizing both observed and unobserved content consistently across viewpoints. Existing camera-control methods for video diffusion typically rely on architectural mechanisms, including camera embeddings, cross-view attention, and geometric conditioning. Although effective for static scene content, these approaches often struggle with the fine-grained, dynamic geometry that must remain coherent across views and over time. We synchronize the diffusion process itself by sampling the Gaussian noise prior in a canonical 3D space derived from the source video and rasterizing it into target cameras, rather than sampling independently in each 2D image plane. Our method, SyncVid, lifts a monocular video into a canonical 4D representation, associates noise with scene geometry and unseen space, and rasterizes the resulting structured noise into arbitrary views. Because rendering can introduce local correlations, we further propose a cross-view-consistent whitening procedure that decorrelates neighboring pixels while preserving correspondence across views. The structured noise serves as the diffusion prior for a LoRA-adapted pretrained video diffusion model, requiring no architectural modifications. By enforcing spatiotemporal and cross-view coherence at the source of stochasticity, SyncVid generates synchronized videos and improves both perceptual quality and cross-view consistency in our experiments. Project page: https://qualcomm-ai-research.github.io/SyncVid-Webpage.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Estimating a camera pose from 3D-2D correspondences - Perspective-n-Point - is one of the most fundamental problems in computer vision. Existing methods either solve a minimal problem or replace the objective with an easier one. Surprisingly, no scalable certifiable solver for the actual Maximum Likelihood Estimation (MLE) exists. We propose PnP*, the first practical solver that guarantees efficient computation and returns a certifiable globally optimal solution. We prove that the underlying convex relaxation is exact under moderate noise, which to our knowledge is the first stability guarantee for any relaxation for the MLE of PnP. Furthermore, we reduce the relaxation by exploiting chordal sparsity pattern and quotient ring reduction such that the relaxation is tractable and scalable. The stability and scalability results are verified numerically, as PnP* returns a provably correct camera pose at over 10 Hz.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generating physically stable 3D structures from visual specifications requires resolving geometric ambiguity while accounting for physical interactions among objects. We present StackGen3D, a framework for generating stable block assemblies conditioned on a given block inventory and three orthographic silhouettes. Our approach consists of two stages. First, we develop a physics-informed graph neural network (PI-GNN) that models support relations between blocks and serves as a fast stability predictor for curating a diverse dataset of stable structures. Second, we train a conditional flow-matching model on this dataset to capture the multimodal distribution of block configurations consistent with the target silhouettes. At inference time, lightweight geometric selection and refinement reduce inter-block penetration without physics simulation. Preliminary experiments show that the PI-GNN generalizes from simple stacks to complex structures substantially better than a parameter-matched multilayer perceptron. For structure generation, Best-of-10 selection improves physical stability from 45.3% to 72.4% while achieving a silhouette IoU of 0.970. These results highlight the potential of combining relational physical reasoning, scalable data curation, and conditional generative modeling for creative 3D assembly.
  Project website: [https://ripl.github.io/StackGen3D-web/](https://ripl.github.io/StackGen3D-web/)

Abstract (英文): Temporary traffic control redirects vehicles through work zones, requiring automated vehicles to connect an interpretation of the visible devices to a continuous passing path. Geometric predictors can provide several plausible paths, yet selecting the one consistent with the temporary control remains difficult. We propose SAGE (Semantic-to-Action Grounded Execution), a read-decide-execute-check framework that separates path construction, path preference, and control compliance within a shared candidate space. The central idea is to use a vision-language model's (VLM's) trajectory as a preference over intact geometric candidates. A role-decoupled generator constructs the candidates, and rank fusion combines each candidate's geometric score with its agreement with the VLM trajectory. Supervision structured around the Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices (MUTCD) trains SAGE-VLM to state the lane status and hazard side before its passage decision and trajectory. The predicted hazard side enters image-space predicates derived from MUTCD provisions, which support a final intervention bounded in score and displacement. On 2,306 real-world ROADWork validation episodes, the candidates include a path within 10 px of the human demonstration in 87% of episodes, yet selection regret accounts for 53% of the geometric ranker's error. SAGE reduces average and final displacement errors by 8.3% and 12.0% relative to the ranker, mostly in the far field, and hard-constraint violations by 37%. The trajectory opinion improves all four tested generator-selector combinations. SAGE is comparable to state-of-the-art VLM modules in accuracy, with a faster-responding VLM, and outperforms four other adapted driving VLMs in displacement error and safety measures. Project page: https://hhjj233.github.io/SAGE-page/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Imaging Flow Cytometry (IFC) generates large multispectral datasets, yet the need for expert annotations bottlenecks the development of supervised deep learning models. While Self-Supervised Learning (SSL) offers a solution, its application to IFC remains fragmented and lacks systematic benchmarking. To address this, we present IFC-SSL-Bench, a modular framework for evaluating SSL in IFC. We benchmark six SSL approaches across four backbones on two heterogeneous datasets. By assessing downstream classification performance across annotation regimes of the training sets (1%-100%), we quantify annotation efficiency and identify optimal configurations for high-dimensional biological imaging. SSL matches the performance of fully supervised baselines trained on 100% of annotated data while using as little as 5% of annotations for morphological classification, with advantages in low-data regimes below 5%. Across both datasets, the contrastive approach MoCo-v2 paired with ConvNeXt backbones emerged as the top-performing configuration, with ConvNeXtV1-T best for immunological synapse classification and ConvNeXtV2-T for morphological classification. We identify a clear pattern in transfer dynamics: ImageNet initialization benefits morphological classification, consistent with its visual similarity to natural images, whereas random initialization better captures the more domain-specific immunological synapse classification. SSL elevates class-specific F1-scores by over 35 percentage points for structurally complex classes at 1% of annotated data. We synthesize these insights into a unified set of guidelines for deploying self-supervised workflows in high-throughput IFC, and release the benchmark as a resource for future work.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Yangtze River Basin is a critical region for freshwater biodiversity conservation. The implementation of the Yangtze River Ten-Year Fishing Ban has increased the importance of effective aquatic biodiversity monitoring and species-level identification. However, fine-grained identification of closely related freshwater fish species remains challenging due to subtle morphological differences. Existing deep learning methods often struggle to balance classification accuracy and inference efficiency, while LLMs may generate unsupported information when producing specialized biological knowledge. To address these challenges, this study proposes FSVL, a fast-slow dual-pathway framework integrating visual recognition with LLM-based Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). A lightweight ResNet50 fast path performs preliminary classification across 51 fish species in the Middle Yangtze River Basin and routes predefined groups of easily confused species to a slow path for refined identification. Guided by taxonomic criteria, the slow path uses YOLOv8n to detect key morphological regions, including the head, dorsal fin, and tail, leveraging morphometric and outline traits to improve discrimination among closely related species. The framework further integrates a retrieval-enhanced Qwen3-14B backend, using soft tokens and Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) for parameter-efficient domain knowledge generation. On the evaluated test set of 607 images, the fast classifier achieved 99.67% accuracy, whereas the cascade corrected the two errors, yielding 100.00% overall accuracy; further validation on independent specimens and external conditions remains needed. Across 255 knowledge generation samples, scientific name accuracy rises from 91.37% to 100.00%, genus accuracy from 90.20% to 98.82%, and ROUGE-L from 0.335 to 0.655, supporting intelligent biodiversity monitoring and fisheries management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Traffic safety videos provide rich evidence for understanding interactions between pedestrians and vehicles, but synthetic-to-real evaluation makes this setting challenging because models must learn from simulated scenes and generalize to real-world traffic. In this work, we present StateBridge, a traffic video-language framework designed to operate under this synthetic-only training protocol. Our approach uses phase-aware clip construction, pedestrian and vehicle track summaries, and structured prompts to ground vision-language models in stable traffic evidence rather than appearance alone. We fine-tune public vision-language models on synthetic captioning and visual question answering examples, then combine their outputs through fact-locked caption fusion and evidence-diverse VQA consensus to improve consistency across actor roles, behavioral phases, and question types. StateBridge achieves an official S2 score of 55.4679 on AI City Challenge 2026 Track 2 using only synthetic training data. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of structured evidence transfer for traffic safety captioning and visual question answering.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Objectives: In the area of digital pathology, nucleus segmentation is an essential step for quantitative analysis; however, high nuclear density and staining heterogeneity in histology images pose a great challenge.
  Methods: This study implements a lightweight Attention U-Net model on the MoNuSeg 2018 dataset to overcome these challenges. The proposed pipeline integrates comprehensive image and mask preprocessing, including stain deconvolution and multi-pass mask smoothing, with a high-fidelity overlapping patch-stitching strategy during inference on high-resolution images.
  Results: The model achieved an Intersection over Union (IoU) score of 0.8745 on full-resolution, unseen test images. This result demonstrates the effectiveness of attention mechanisms in enhancing feature localization and validates the stitching methodology for generating seamless and accurate segmentation maps.
  Conclusion: Attention U-Net is an effective and competitive method for automated, scalable nucleus delineation in challenging clinical contexts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present PixGR, an efficient framework that integrates lightweight pixel-space generative modeling into feed-forward 3D Gaussian reconstruction to model scene uncertainty. Context observations are encoded once into a scene latent representation, which conditions generative decoding from noisy target views to produce a shared 3D Gaussian representation. We formulate this process using pixel-space flow matching, enabling end-to-end optimization of 3D reconstruction with direct pixel-space generative supervision through differentiable rendering. This avoids VAE-based latent compression and error propagation inherent in previous two-stage pipelines. To preserve feed-forward efficiency, PixGR caches the scene representation and performs iterative inference using a lightweight DiT decoder. Experiments demonstrate that PixGR outperforms regression-based approaches in challenging sparse-view settings, particularly with widely spaced observations and view extrapolation. Meanwhile, it maintains sub-second inference and remains competitive with substantially heavier pretrained generative models. Our project page is available at https://ranrhuang.github.io/pixgr/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Text-based person anomaly search requires a model to retrieve individuals from a large image gallery using natural-language descriptions of both appearance and behavior. This task is challenging because model training relies on synthetic image-text pairs, whereas evaluation is performed on real surveillance images containing domain differences, near-duplicate frames, and many visually similar distractors. We present a multi-model retrieval and reranking framework designed to improve synthetic-to-real transfer. The framework first adapts Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B to synthetic person descriptions using bidirectional contrastive learning, explicit negative sampling, false-negative masking, and domain randomization. It then combines the normalized scores of six complementary Qwen3-VL checkpoints with those of a frozen SigLIP2 model using four-view gallery encoding. The fused retrieval scores identify the strongest candidates, which are jointly evaluated with the query using Qwen3-VL-Reranker-8B. The cross-encoder and retrieval scores are subsequently blended to produce the final ranking. We also construct a distractor-matched validation protocol entirely from synthetic data to select checkpoint weights, fusion coefficients, candidate depths, and reranking parameters without accessing the official test images. The final system achieves 94.7815% mAP and 90.8493% R@1 under the official AI City Challenge 2026 Track 4 test setting, with a reported eighth-place finish on the public leaderboard.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent instruction-following image editors such as Flux.1-Kontext enable text-driven editing but lack fine-grained control over where and how much an edit is applied. Existing approaches provide either continuous but global control or spatially localized editing at a fixed strength. To our knowledge, none jointly supports explicit spatial localization, continuous per-region strength, and independent simultaneous control of multiple subjects. We present LOCI (LOcalized Continuous control for multi- subject Image editing). Given a multi-subject image with per-subject regions and instructions, LOCI edits each subject at an independently chosen, continuously adjustable strength, for instance gradually aging one person while adding a beard to another with minimal cross-region leakage. LOCI pairs subject-level attention binding for spatial isolation with a Regional LoRA layer that replaces the LoRA's scalar coefficient with a per-token scaling vector aligned to the spatial layout. A single adapter is trained at one suppression point, and continuous edit strengths follow by rescaling its regional residual at inference. Experiments on multi-subject scenes demonstrate that LOCI achieves strong edit fidelity with significantly reduced cross-region interference compared to baselines, while maintaining continuous control over edit strength

</details>

Abstract (英文): Surgical point tracking requires persistent correspondence through tissue deformation and occlusion, yet dense trajectory annotations are costly to obtain. We present STEP, a two-stage framework that learns from surgical video and sparse physical endpoints. First, a frozen offline teacher generates candidate trajectories, and temporal reversibility and stereo consistency select dense supervision for a causal student. Second, a robust physical endpoint loss anchors terminal correspondence, while a round-trip loss constrains temporal consistency. A gradient-free prefix exposes training to accumulated drift, while a final differentiable window bounds activation memory. The trained tracker processes a single RGB stream with fixed weights. In STIR-to-VL-SurgPT transfer, temporal-stereo union selection reduces endpoint error by 7.0% relative to unfiltered trajectories. On a post-hoc diagnostic of 29 VL-SurgPT clips excluded from the endpoint-training manifest, STEP reduces endpoint error from 31.52 to 16.80 pixels relative to dense-label fine-tuning. These results support combining curated trajectories with physical observations to train surgical point trackers.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deepfakes increasingly undermine trust in digital media, yet existing detectors often struggle to generalize across unseen manipulation techniques, compression levels, and dataset shifts. Most approaches rely on RGB appearance, frequency-domain artifacts, or their combination, learning cues present in the final synthesized image. While effective in-distribution, such cues are frequently generator-specific and can diminish as synthesis models evolve. We argue that a complementary perspective can be obtained through implicit neural representations (INRs), which expose how an image is reconstructed rather than merely how it appears. By fitting an INR to an image and analyzing its internal activations, one can reveal reconstruction dynamics that are difficult to observe in the image domain alone and may capture more intrinsic differences between genuine and synthetic content. To this end, we propose Spectra, a deepfake detection framework that combines semantic visual representations with artifact-aware cues derived from INR reconstruction. Spectra extracts multi-level reconstruction heatmaps from image-specific INRs and jointly models them with semantic features through a bidirectional fusion mechanism. This enables direct interaction between image semantics and reconstruction artifacts, allowing the model to identify forgery cues that are less dependent on specific generation pipelines. Extensive experiments on multiple deepfake benchmarks demonstrate improved robustness and cross-dataset generalization, consistently outperforming detectors based solely on RGB appearance or semantic representations. Furthermore, we show that the proposed representation extends beyond deepfake detection to face presentation attack detection, highlighting its potential as a unified framework for facial attack detection.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video encoder models are increasingly used as world models for physical AI, where a policy plans by rolling out the latent, so what physics the latent holds bounds what the agent can plan over. Moreover, existing interpretability work probes quantities belonging to a single object; the quantities that make physics relational, such as momentum transfer, restitution, and friction, are defined between bodies and have not been examined. We probe relational physics attributes in two frozen self-supervised video encoders (V-JEPA2 and VideoMAE) under two-body settings and test if they transfer across environments. Our early findings are mixed. Both encoders separate elastic from inelastic collisions reliably. V-JEPA2's own predictor is less likely to predict a collision that creates kinetic energy compared to one that loses it. Momentum-violation detection appears in only one of the two encoders, a kinetic-energy readout survives a change of environment for V-JEPA2 but not for VideoMAE, and neither model holds a friction coefficient that survives a change of geometry.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision language models (VLMs) have been increasingly adopted in consumer smart-home camera systems to interpret recorded videos and provide insights. However, video clips captured from smart-home cameras are different from curated web videos dominating existing VLM benchmarks. Smart-home video clips often do not contain subtitles or narration and are captured from different viewing angles, under various lighting conditions, and in diverse scenarios. However, the capabilities of the model in this domain, under sparse visual signals and limited context, are not fully assessed by existing VLM benchmarks. To close this gap, we introduce OpenHome, a visual-only, four-option multiple-choice benchmark for evaluating smart-home video understanding. It contains 2,254 questions on 1,118 real-world video clips and organizes 10 tasks into three capability levels. Perception evaluates visible content, Reasoning evaluates causal, spatial, and temporal relationships, and Insight evaluates subject intent, supporting evidence and appropriate homeowner responses. Based on the dataset, we describe the task design, data pipeline and evaluation protocol, and report results for 32 closed-source and open-weight VLMs. The best model, Gemini 4 Argon, reaches 81.2% overall accuracy. Frontier closed-source models outperform open-weight models on every level. The best closed-source model leads the best open-weight model by 16.3% on Perception, 22.4% on Reasoning and 37.4% on Insight.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This paper studies the problem of test-time robustness scaling for large vision-language models (LVLMs), which aims to mitigate language bias during inference. Existing approaches typically adopt test-time scaling techniques such as contrastive decoding to improve inference performance. Despite the progress, the performance of these approaches is far from satisfactory due to intentional evidence removal during perturbations and redundant inference cost. Towards this end, we propose a novel approach named Margin-gated Contrastive Debiasing with Evidence Restoration (MAGIC). The core of our MAGIC is to evaluate the decision margins of LVLMs to selectively activate textual and visual perturbation branches for efficient debiased inference. In particular, our MAGIC first measures the prediction margins of crucial tokens, and applies a gating function to identify low-confidence responses that are susceptible to language biases. For selected samples, we integrate the token logits from multiple textual and image perturbation branches with the original ones for debiased decision. More importantly, we selectively restore the most informative corrupted views with sharp prediction distributions for ambiguous samples after debiasing, enabling evidence recovery for discriminative decision-making. We further utilize a calibration dataset to actively identify and prune ineffective branches during contrastive debiasing for cost reduction. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets validate the effectiveness of the proposed MAGIC in comparison with extensive baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision Transformers (ViTs) have demonstrated strong performance in many computer vision tasks but do not naturally extend beyond the natural image domain. One important example is Fourier-space data, which is used to represent a rich and diverse set of modalities, including medical imaging and microscopy. Standard ViT tokenization partitions an image into square patches on a Cartesian grid, which poorly fit the frequency and phase structure of Fourier-space data. We introduce Non-Uniform ViT, a simple, general-purpose vision transformer that redesigns image tokenization, data representation, and positional encoding to respect this structure. Experiments on Fourier-domain image classification, 2D crystal lattice identification, MRI annotation, and cryo-EM protein heterogeneity analysis show that NUViT consistently captures the structure of Fourier data better than standard architectures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Text-to-image (T2I) models have achieved remarkable progress in image quality and instruction following, prompting growing research interest in their capacity for deep understanding that goes beyond literal prompt matching. However, without an explicit visual scheme provided, it remains unclear and has not been systematically evaluated whether T2I models can autonomously translate abstract concepts lacking a fixed visual referent, such as "loyalty," into visual representations that are accurate, diverse, and distinguishable from closely related concepts. We define this capability as Abstract Concept Visualization Ability (ACVA) and introduce ACVA-Bench, a category-balanced benchmark grounded in psycholinguistic norms of abstract concepts, which examines models' ACVA along three dimensions semantic sufficiency, representational diversity, and conceptual distinctiveness under a low-intervention setting that withholds concrete objects, scenes, or visual metaphors. Since abstract concepts lack a unique reference image, conventional evaluation metrics that rely on reference-image matching are difficult to apply. To address this, we propose a fine-grained visual-semantic evaluation method constrained by concept semantics, which decomposes concept semantics into independently verifiable visual-semantic questions to assess the semantic representation quality of generated images, with its validity further confirmed by human evaluation. Benchmarking results on mainstream T2I models show that these models rely on a small set of prototypical visual patterns, leaving room for improvement in representational diversity and conceptual distinctiveness for abstract concepts, thereby providing an interpretable and reproducible evaluation foundation for future research.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion-based image generation models produce high-quality images but may generate harmful or policy-violating content, motivating machine unlearning (MU) to selectively remove target concepts while preserving general capabilities. However, existing MU approaches often struggle with the distributed and entangled nature of concept representations, leading to inefficient optimization and imprecise edits when applied through large-scale fine-tuning over full diffusion trajectories. To bridge this gap, we propose Concept Acupuncturist (ConceptACP), a novel MU framework that integrates interpretability-guided analysis with inference-free fine-tuning for efficient and precise concept removal. By establishing mappings between semantic concepts and cross-attention blocks in the U-Net denoiser and localizing the most concept-relevant block for targeted optimization, ConceptACP avoids full diffusion trajectory optimization, thereby improving fine-tuning efficiency. To mitigate concept entanglement, we propose a feature-level correction mechanism based on Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs), where SAE-derived sparse, semantically meaningful feature activations are manipulated to construct targeted correction signals, which are then projected back to the output space of the corresponding block and used as supervision for precise concept removal. Consequently, ConceptACP eliminates the need for external inference-time modules while achieving efficient and fine-grained unlearning. Instantiated on SDXL-Turbo, ConceptACP improves the forgetting success rate by 36.34 percentage points in the standard one-step generation setting on average over the best-performing baseline, while preserving image quality and prompt fidelity, and generalizes effectively across other Stable Diffusion backbones. Our code is available at https://github.com/Eran-Xia/ConceptACP.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Despite the promising development of deep learning for video anomaly detection (VAD), explainability (XAI) of these models remains a challenge. Explainable and privacy-protecting VAD builds transparency and trust that facilitates adoption of computer vision systems, including safety and security monitoring. However, anomaly explainability in privacy-preserving VAD systems is underexplored. In this study, we present the XAVAD approach, a unified descriptive anomaly XAI for an anonymized VAD, that integrates flexible zero-shot anonymization, anomaly type categorization, visual localization, and scene context description. We provide empirical and qualitative assessments of multifaceted explanations integrated with full-body visual anonymization for privacy protection. We adopt a multimodal CLIP embedding for videos, using improved LLM-assisted prompting and pool-scoring mechanisms for anomaly-type identification. We enhance Grad-ECLIP for spatial anomaly heatmap localization, leveraging computation and accuracy through our novel prompt-guided patching autofocus mechanism. We employ VLMs to generate context and reasoning descriptions of detected anomalies through curated prompt engineering. We have evaluated the accuracy and computational cost of XAVAD on several publicly available CCTV and video datasets. Our experiments demonstrate substantial gains of our XAVAD over state-of-the-art benchmark approaches and provide insights into privacy protection trade-offs in XAI for VAD. We further present ablation studies and assessments across diverse configurations and modeling alternatives to demonstrate the efficacy of our proposed methods. Abstract (英文): Feed-forward neural renderers synthesize global illumination across novel 3D scenes without per-scene optimization or runtime ray tracing. Existing transformer-based renderers tokenize individual triangles, so self-attention scales quadratically with mesh resolution. Yet macroscopic radiative transport is governed by the underlying surface geometry and object arrangements rather than a particular mesh discretization; finer tessellation can thus inflate token counts without changing the underlying transport physics, motivating a discretization-agnostic and object-centric representation. We introduce RADIANT (RADiance-based Inter-Actor Neural Transformer), an object-centric architecture for feed-forward global illumination from point inputs. By representing each object with a compact budget of 32 surface-patch tokens, RADIANT decouples attention sequence length from polygon counts. In a single network, an inter-object scene transformer models 3D radiative exchange and a view decoder renders images at target camera poses. To capture light transport without materializing O(N^2) bias matrices, invariant form-factor attention embeds geometric distances and surface orientations directly into query-key dot products as SE(3)-invariant inner products weighted by learned area and material gains. For primary visibility without mesh connectivity, camera rays cross-attend scene tokens guided by depth-tested point splats in a shared spherical frame. Across a 1.03M-scene benchmark under matched model capacity, RADIANT reaches 25 dB tone-mapped PSNR in 5x fewer training steps than RenderFormer, and achieves 32.2 dB at 600k steps (+3.3 dB over RenderFormer and +7.6 dB over RenderFormer-V2). We further demonstrate the scalability of RADIANT, which completes a 512x512 forward pass of a 500-object scene in 0.83 s, with computational cost scaling with the number of objects rather than polygons.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Complex text-to-image requests remain difficult to satisfy reliably. Verifying and repairing an image can fix an individual output, but a successful repair does not necessarily carry over to other requests. We present ImageRSI, a framework that keeps the image generator frozen and combines three components: five-dimensional verification and repair within each task, an experience library shared across tasks, and offline updating of the judge. Candidate experiences enter the library only after paired A/B validation, and the judge is updated with supervised fine-tuning and GRPO on accumulated image-judgment records and evaluated before reuse. We also introduce ImageReq-Bench, a benchmark of 961 requests built around hard cases in real-world image-generation requests, each paired with an evaluation checklist. ImageRSI achieves a Checklist Strict Accuracy (CSA) of 52.6% on MindBench (with Nano Banana Pro as the generator) and 65.66% on ImageReq-Bench, and we compare system outcomes across judge-training configurations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): RVL-CDIP is the de facto standard benchmark for document classification, and reported accuracy on it is now 98.8%. In this paper, we audit RVL-CDIP for data quality issues and find that approximately 10.5% of its 400,000 documents have erroneous labels and that 35.2% of the test set has a duplicate or near-duplicate in the training set. Identification codes on the pages are sufficient to classify at 69% accuracy, and a state-of-the-art model loses 11 points without them. Image-only models trained on RVL-CDIP lose 16 to 28 points on documents from other sources, and the choice of OCR engine changes accuracy by 4.1 to 8.3 points. We find that nearly 16,000 RVL-CDIP documents contain sensitive personal data, and produce de-identified versions of these documents. We re-benchmark 25 models, and with all defects remediated, the 13 that we retrain are 0.9 to 2.9 points more accurate than in the original condition; the most accurate of them, LayoutLMv3, is 96.5% accurate. We release the corrected corpus, the annotations and the out-of-distribution data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Proactive streaming video understanding requires assistants to track unfolding events and respond when a user's requested condition is met. To make these decisions, assistants must accumulate relevant evidence and reassess response conditions as new observations arrive. Existing work offers complementary capabilities for this process: proactive video assistants learn when to respond, while streaming video reasoning methods generate scheduled intermediate text to organize observations and support subsequent answers. Together, these capabilities provide a foundation for proactive reasoning, but how to coordinate ongoing reasoning with timely responses remains underexplored. In a direct adaptation, fixed reasoning intervals can trigger updates when nothing relevant has changed or postpone useful updates until the next scheduled step. Sharing one generation path also couples two distinct requirements: preserving evidence for future decisions and producing only the response requested by the user. Moreover, responses generated after reasoning must wait for it to finish, even when sufficient evidence is already available. To address these challenges, we introduce SyncPro, a synchronous dual-stream framework with specialized generation paths for evidence tracking and user-facing responses. Its Inner and Response branches share causal history and activate independently, allowing relevant evidence changes to trigger internal updates and responses to proceed concurrently with ongoing reasoning. We construct 189K dual-stream examples and train SyncPro through supervised fine-tuning followed by trajectory-level reinforcement learning. Experiments across three proactive benchmarks show improved response timing and more accurate answers to user requests.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperbolic vision-language models are well suited to modeling the hierarchical structure of multimodal data, because hyperbolic volume grows exponentially with radius, enabling low-distortion representations of tree-like taxonomies from general to specific concepts. However, existing pretraining objectives for hyperbolic vision-language models entangle semantic alignment with entailment modeling, forcing radial coordinates to simultaneously encode semantic matching and hierarchical structure, leading to conflicting geometric constraints. In this paper, we introduce AURORA, a hyperbolic vision-language framework that decouples the objectives through angular alignment and a novel Radial-Angular Order Cone (RAOC) for entailment modeling. Angular alignment captures semantic similarity while preserving the radial structure for representing hierarchies. Unlike conventional entailment cones, RAOC determines angular allowance from relative parent-child radial gaps. It is incorporated into hierarchical entailment contrastive training to capture partial-order relations. We further introduce a norm regularization term that prevents undesirable radial degeneration for stable training. We train models on millions of image-text pairs and evaluate them across zero-shot classification, image-text retrieval, hierarchical reasoning, and compositional understanding. Our model consistently outperforms strong Euclidean and hyperbolic vision-language baselines, demonstrating the effectiveness of decoupling semantic alignment from hierarchical entailment modeling.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Affective computing is an interdisciplinary field that studies computational methods that relate to or influence emotion. These methods have been applied to interactive media artworks, but they have focused on affect detection rather than affect generation. For affect generation, computationally creative methods need to be explored that have recently been driven by the use of Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), a deep learning method. The experiment presented in this paper, Forging Emotions, explores the use of visual emotion datasets and the working processes of GANs for visual affect generation, that is, for generating images that can convey or trigger specified emotions. This experiment concludes that the methodology used so far by computer science researchers to build image datasets for describing high-level concepts such as emotions is insufficient and proposes utilizing emotional networks of associations according to psychology research. Forging Emotions also concludes that to generate affect visually, merely corresponding to basic psychology findings, such as bright or dark colours, does not seem adequate. Therefore, research efforts should aim to understand the structure of trained GANs and compositional GANs in order to produce genuinely novel compositions that can convey or trigger emotions through the subject matter of generated images.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated monitoring of dairy cows increasingly measures one part of the body, such as the rump for temperature or the back for posture, yet the region itself is usually placed by hand. Existing systems either score the cow as a whole or detect sparse keypoints, often in controlled walkways, and neither yields the named region masks that such measurements need on a crowded commercial rotary platform. We present a perception front end that finds the target cow, ten bony landmarks and four named dorsal regions (withers, back, rump and tail-head) in a single forward pass over a whole RGB-D frame from a commercial rotary parlour. Two encoders, one for colour and one for depth, are fused late and share one decoder with three heads. On an anatomical region dataset that we built on public rotary-parlour RGB-D images (538 annotated frames, cow-disjoint test set), the network reaches PCK@10 0.905 for landmarks and region IoU 0.70 to 0.91, and runs end to end in 26.4 ms per frame on a T4 GPU, against an 11 fps camera. Dense region supervision is what makes the landmarks accurate: removing the region head costs 0.15 PCK@10. Agreement between the two heads flags unreliable frames without labels. On our own rig at the largest dairy farm in Western Australia, the model transfers with ten labelled frames (PCK@10 0.84), and its regions are carried through depth into a radiometric thermal camera, giving one repeatable temperature per region and cow.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are widely deployed, yet the internal mechanisms behind their safety behavior remain poorly understood. We present a lightweight inference-time framework for localizing and manipulating safety-sensitive representations in frozen VLMs, without retraining or modifying parameters. On Qwen3-VL-4B, layer-wise activation divergence analysis reveals non-uniform safety geometry across depth. Causal interventions show that early vision layers support strong bidirectional refusal control through activation steering along a learned safety direction, with unsafe rejection spanning 6%-96%, whereas later layers respond more to activation magnitude scaling and exhibit a substantial safety-selectivity trade-off. Deeper layers remain well separated along the safety direction yet are largely insensitive to steering, distinguishing representational separability from causal controllability. InternVL2.5-4B shows qualitatively similar depth-dependent activation geometry despite differences in layer location and magnitude. Our results expose a white-box, representation-level refusal-bypass attack surface in VLM vision encoders and motivate inference-time stabilization of safety-sensitive representations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing image change captioning (RSICC) describes in natural language the semantic changes between two images of the same scene captured at different times. Previous methods commonly merge the pair during repeated processing or combine intra-image and cross-image relations in one operation, weakening source identity and leaving the two temporal directions implicit. Without separate modules for intra-image and cross-temporal processing and a separate path for each direction, the contribution of each part cannot be measured on its own, so it remains unclear which part matters most, and the design of later models has no such evidence to build on. To address these issues, we propose DS-BiCA-Net, which assigns the two kinds of processing to separate modules and keeps the two images as separate streams throughout the encoder. A windowed state-space module models context within each image, and a bidirectional cross-attention module compares the two images along two directed paths that share their projections. The two modules use the same pre-normalisation residual architecture and differ only in their mixers, and the two streams are fused only in the aggregation head, so each module and each direction can be removed and its contribution measured on its own. Controlled experiments on LEVIR-CC and Dubai-CC examine operator interaction, temporal direction and module order, showing that intra-image refinement carries no benefit alone and becomes beneficial only when followed by bidirectional cross-attention, an order that outperforms the reverse at matched parameter count and FLOPs. The bidirectional module also outperforms either temporal direction alone. Our method reaches an S_m^* of 82.65 on LEVIR-CC, the highest among the published results compared, and 66.72 on Dubai-CC without geometric augmentation, the second-highest among the published results compared.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Gradient-based learning of morphological operators is hindered by locally sparse derivatives with respect to the structuring elements. We develop a method for learning rank filters that retains exact operator evaluation while distributing the derivatives across the window. Using threshold decomposition, we construct a neural disjunctive normal form representation and establish a sufficient condition for exactness with integer-valued images and structuring elements. We then derive a smooth surrogate from the unique root of a smooth threshold equation. Implicit differentiation gives closed-form derivatives with normalised local weights, whose distribution is controlled by a smoothing parameter. These derivatives are used in a straight-through procedure: the forward pass evaluates the exact rank filter, while the backward pass uses the surrogate. For fixed ranks, the procedure learns real-valued structuring elements of any rank filter, including the median, and extends to compositions, infima, and suprema of rank filters. Experiments on image denoising using MNIST and Fashion-MNIST and retinal vessel segmentation show that, starting from a generic random initialisation, the proposed approach consistently improves performance over training with exact subgradients in every setting. In image denoising, our best network also outperforms smooth morphological networks in all settings. On vessel segmentation, the learned erosions recover structuring elements that fit the vessels, as theoretically expected, whereas most of those trained with exact subgradients remain close to their initialisation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Continual survival analysis on whole-slide images (WSIs) requires models to integrate knowledge from sequentially arriving cancer cohorts while preserving previously learned information. Model merging offers a data-free alternative by combining independently fine-tuned task experts without retaining previous training data. However, the relative behavior of different merging strategies for WSI-based survival prediction remains insufficiently understood. In this work, we establish an end-to-end benchmark for data-free continual model merging, covering task-specific fine-tuning, sequential merging, and inference under both TASK-IL and CLASS-IL settings. We systematically evaluate six representative merging strategies across four TCGA cancer cohorts and three task orders using C-index, IPCW C-index, Forgetting, and Backward Transfer under a controlled protocol. Results show that no merging strategy consistently dominates across all evaluation criteria, while censoring-aware evaluation can substantially alter the relative ranking of the compared methods. This benchmark provides a unified foundation for developing and evaluating more effective continual model merging methods for WSI-based survival analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Autoregressive vision-language models (VLMs) struggle to ground generated text in the correct image regions, and existing explainability methods, such as attention maps and Logit Lens, often produce poor spatial explanations due to weak text-to-vision attention and sink tokens. We propose \textbf{Angular Relevance} ($R_L$), a simple visualization measure based on cosine similarity between visual token embeddings and unembedding-matrix rows. Because $R_L$ is invariant to token norm, it produces substantially sharper and more faithful heatmaps than attention maps or Logit Lens on out-of-the-box models. Without any training, $R_L$ yields competitive referring-expression segmentation (RES), outperforming prior diagnostic maps and CLIP-based training-free methods. To preserve localized object information during cross-modal mixing, we introduce \textbf{Angular Object-Aware Loss (AOL)}, an auxiliary loss that directly supervises visual token embeddings to align angularly with the vocabulary directions of their corresponding object concepts. Finetuning with AOL alongside the standard next-token prediction objective significantly sharpens object localization in heatmaps, showing that the VLM's own visual tokens can be used directly for grounding and improving RES, without training any segmentation heads and without degrading general VQA performance. We further demonstrate that the learned grounding also transfers to category-level segmentation of object categories not seen during training.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reinforcement learning has gained attention for improving action-conditioned world models (ACWMs), as it enables reward-driven optimization of visual quality (VQ) and action-following fidelity (AF) beyond flow-matching optimization. Realizing this potential requires reward signals that reliably rank videos by both VQ and AF. However, existing rewards capture only one dimension: metric-based rewards assess VQ but require ground-truth videos and do not directly assess AF, while inverse dynamics model (IDM) rewards assess AF without references but do not guarantee VQ. Moreover, IDMs, typically trained on real videos, may provide unreliable rewards for generated videos containing visual artifacts and physical inconsistencies. In this work, we introduce the Action and Video Mismatch Benchmark (AVM-Eval), comprising 67.2k pairs that evaluate how reliably IDMs assess AF on real and generated videos. We then propose ACWM-RM, a reward model that jointly assesses VQ and AF without ground-truth videos during reward computation. To train it, we first construct the Action and Video Mismatch Preference Dataset (AVM-Pref) with 506.3k pairs providing complementary supervision: action-mismatch pairs supervise AF, while video-mismatch pairs supervise VQ and expose the model to generated videos. We then pretrain an action-conditioned predictor on real videos to learn forward dynamics before jointly training it with a patch-weighted score head on AVM-Pref to transfer these dynamics to AF assessment on generated videos. Evaluation on AVM-Eval reveals the limited reliability of IDMs on generated videos, while downstream RL experiments show that ACWM-RM outperforms reward baselines in both criteria.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Modern autonomous driving systems widely adopt multi-view Bird's-Eye-View (BEV) based 3D perception models due to their superior performance. Despite their success, the robustness of these models against adversarial camouflage attacks remains largely unexplored. Existing camouflage attacks primarily target single-view 2D detectors, limiting their effectiveness in multi-view 3D perception settings. To address this gap, we propose BEVCA, a novel adversarial camouflage generation framework tailored for multi-view BEV-based 3D perception. Our framework integrates a BEV-feature-based adversarial loss with a multi-view neural rendering module, enabling effective and transferable camouflage attacks across different models and tasks. Extensive experiments in both digital and physical settings demonstrate that BEVCA outperforms state-of-the-art baselines and exhibits strong robustness under real-world conditions. Abstract (英文): Fixed-window Vision Transformer (ViT) decoders run at deployment resolution as overlapping tiles: every token in an overlap band is processed by several full decoder instances. In the 2.4B-parameter MiniMax-H3 video decoder, about half of all per-token computation is duplicated. Removing the tiles fails because attention, global tokens and coordinates are bound to them. TileShare keeps each released tile's attention and computes every other operation once per token. The released computation changes at one kind of step only: an overlap token's per-tile results are blended into one shared state with the released weights. Interventions show that this blend costs quality where attention reads the shared states, mainly in the first few layers, which TileShare therefore keeps per tile. Without training, keeping six layers per tile decodes 1.38-1.51x faster than batched and compiled tiling on two GPU types (up to 1.96x with FP8) and is equivalent to the released decoder in PSNR, LPIPS and DISTS on DAVIS videos. On unseen UVG videos with the setting frozen beforehand, it matches PSNR and DISTS and improves LPIPS. TileShare carries over to two further decoders and is again confirmed on unseen data. It is equivalent in PSNR and DISTS on MAGI-1. On the ViTok-v2 image autoencoder at 2K-4K, it matches overlapping tiling in PSNR, improves LPIPS and DISTS and decodes 1.5-1.8x faster. How many layers stay per tile is measured once per model (for decoders, without labels); we report it for 14 decoders and encoders. Optionally, two GPU-hours of self-distillation let MiniMax-H3 share every layer without a loss in PSNR, and a coordinate field trained while the network stays frozen brings fully shared CLIP-based open-vocabulary segmentation to parity with sliding windows on seven benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Single-image 3D scene construction is a fundamental problem in 3D vision. Recent methods such as SAM-3Dfy enable end-to-end reconstruction of 3D assets and their scene layouts, but still struggle with fine-grained scene geometry and asset quality. Meanwhile, specialized 3D object generators provide detailed object geometry, while 3D foundation models offer strong and generalizable spatial understanding. We propose Easy-3Dfy, a compositional workflow that decouples object generation from scene layout estimation, allowing specialized models to fully exploit their respective strengths. An RPF-based aligner then bridges these capabilities by establishing geometric correspondence between object-local meshes and partial scene pointmaps, recovering object layout for scene composition. Without requiring complete scene-level supervision, the proposed workflow consistently outperforms SAM-3Dfy across multiple geometric metrics on Scene-Maker and 3D-FUTURE200. Our results demonstrate that explicit geometric composition can effectively combine high-quality object generation with strong spatial understanding, while enabling the workflow to benefit independently from advances in both 3D object generation and foundation models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Distilling semantic meaning from raw image data remains a challenge in machine learning. Current self-supervised paradigms face a trade-off: joint-embedding architectures produce relevant features by aggressively discarding intra-class variance, irreversibly spatial and reconstruction. Conversely, generative models preserve spatial details but struggle to isolate semantics, as their optimization is dominated by pixel variations. To bridge this divide, we propose composite subspace flow matching (CSFM). Rather than corrupting data with unstructured isotropic noise, CSFM formulates representation learning as the variance-minimizing inversion of a deterministic degradation curriculum across spectral, color, and illumination subspaces. By bounding trajectory variance with hard temporal style truncation, a capacity-constrained encoder is driven to ignore transient style artifacts and encode persistent content. Empirical evaluations across CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and STL-10 show that CSFM yields competitive discriminative transferability while preserving generative synthesis across both convolutional and vision transformer architectures. By avoiding the collapse pathologies of non-generative methods, CSFM forms a smooth latent space that outperforms standard optimal transport flows on linear probing and few-shot classification, while enabling coherent synthesis and trajectory interpolation. These results confirm that steering the physical dynamics of generative flows offers a scalable paradigm for learning representations that are simultaneously linearly separable and generatively invertible.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D MLLMs recognize objects via pattern matching, which fails when geometrically similar objects require functional reasoning to disambiguate (e.g., dresser vs. wardrobe). Our per-sample Geometric Confusion Score (GCS) exposes this failure: baseline accuracy is 33.7% on ModelNet40's most ambiguous 20% versus 84.0% on its least ambiguous 20%. We identify a function-as-annotation pattern, in which functional language decorates a known identity instead of supporting inference, and hypothesize that reversing this information flow should concentrate improvements on the most ambiguous samples. PFIR (Part-level Functional Inference for Recognition) reverses that flow. Built on PartNeXt part segmentation, its data pipeline produces 507K QA pairs with functional CoTs (geometry $\to$ function $\to$ identity), and its progressive curriculum culminates in dual-objective GRPO: strict functional reasoning and descriptive grounding for Objaverse's diverse distribution. The two RL objectives yield nearly orthogonal task vectors ($\cos = 0.056$), enabling composition via Task Arithmetic. Under a strict dual OOD protocol (Objaverse $\to$ ModelNet40; XYZRGB $\to$ XYZ-only), PFIR raises zero-shot accuracy from 60.5% to 66.7%, with gains concentrated $2.6\times$ more on geometrically ambiguous samples---supporting part-level functional reasoning as an effective route to more generalizable 3D recognition. Code: https://github.com/this233/pfir-3d.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Artificial intelligence-based analysis of intraoperative laparoscopic videos has the potential to improve surgical decision-making and precision. However, effective surgical assistance requires models that can generalize across diverse intraoperative tasks. Here we present LapaFM, a foundation model for laparoscopic surgery trained on more than 20,000 minutes of surgical videos using a multi-stage continual learning strategy that mitigates catastrophic forgetting and inter-task interference. LapaFM learns multimodal visual representations and supports multiple tasks, including smoke removal, surgical phase recognition, instrument and polyp segmentation, and lesion identification. The model was evaluated on 13 retrospective and two prospective cohorts collected from different imaging devices. LapaFM consistently outperformed task-specific models and existing foundation models across datasets, and also generalized to gastrointestinal endoscopy tasks, including polyp detection and classification. Notably, it recovered over 90% of smoke-obscured frames and achieved clinician-level or superior performance in lymph node metastasis prediction and prospective polyp classification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Class imbalance and errors near anatomical boundaries remain challenges in medical image segmentation, motivating loss functions that account for these difficulties. Adaptive t-vMF Dice Loss adjusts the similarity function during training but requires hyperparameter tuning. We propose Self-Tuned Boundary-Aware t-vMF Dice Loss (STB-tvMF Dice), which adapts the function separately for boundary and interior regions without tuning shape-adaptation parameters. Each region's shape parameter of t-vMF function is determined from its mean prediction-target cosine similarity in the preceding epoch and remains fixed throughout the current epoch. This rule maximizes the slope of the similarity function at the observed mean similarity, providing a principled basis for adaptation without validation-based parameter selection. Experiments on three datasets and architectures demonstrated its effectiveness. On Synapse with U-Net, STB-tvMF Dice achieved a mean DSC of 70.75%, compared with 63.03% for Adaptive t-vMF Dice Loss. Our code will be available soon.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing temporal video grounding (TVG) benchmarks primarily focus on localizing target video segments according to descriptive text queries of the visual content. However, real-world queries are more diverse and often require the model to first understand the user's intent before localization, such as finding visual evidence, identifying the cause of an event, or recalling a remembered moment, yet such queries remain largely overlooked. To mitigate this gap, we introduce RVG-Bench, a benchmark for reasoning-driven audio-visual temporal grounding, where every query is constructed such that the target segment can only be identified through reasoning about the query's underlying intent. We carefully construct the benchmark and rigorously verify its data quality to ensure models truly comprehend the audio-visual content. RVG-Bench comprises 1,255 videos and 1,890 query-interval pairs across eight reasoning types. We evaluate 22 models and find that the strongest, Qwen3.8-Omni-Flash, achieves only 49.6 mIoU. Through a series of controlled analyses, including oracle-grounding probes and test-time scaling experiments, we systematically examine where and why current models struggle, providing insights for future model development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Quality inspection of sugar beets after harvest and storage is critical to preserve sugar content and ensure optimal processing efficiency. The transfer of harvested beets to field clamps or trucks provides an opportunity to visually inspect individual beets automatically on loader conveyors. However, this is a challenging task as the large number of beets causes partial overlap and occlusion, complicating both annotation and detection. In this work, we propose an end-to-end image-based pipeline for detecting partially occluded sugar beets in loading scenarios as a first step toward automated quality inspection. We introduce a dedicated benchmark and present an automatic annotation strategy that leverages the SAM 3 foundation model to generate bounding boxes. We compare models trained on manually annotated and automatically generated labels. Additionally, we investigate deployment considerations by benchmarking multiple inference configurations, including execution on an edge device, under realistic operational constraints regarding precision, inference time and power consumption. Our results show that, despite substantial occlusion and inherent annotation ambiguity, foundation-model-based labels can substitute for a substantial share of manual annotation, trading labeling effort for data volume at a modest accuracy cost. Furthermore, we demonstrate that a Raspberry Pi 5 equipped with a Hailo accelerator enables high-performance, low-power inference, making it a practical solution for mobile outdoor deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Early detection of colorectal polyps is essential for preventing colorectal cancer, making accurate polyp segmentation a key component in computer-aided diagnosis systems. However, existing endoscopic segmentation methods often exhibit limited representational capacity and insufficient integration of global and local contextual information. To address these limitations, we propose Polyp-VSSD, a novel local-global joint decoding framework built upon a pre-trained Transformer backbone for high-precision polyp segmentation. Polyp-VSSD leverages the strong feature expressiveness of large-scale visual pre-training and unifies global semantic modeling with local fine-grained reconstruction to enhance segmentation fidelity. Extensive experiments on multiple public endoscopic datasets demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both Dice and IoU. On the EndoScene dataset, Polyp-VSSD achieves mIoU and mDice scores of 0.903 and 0.946, respectively, highlighting its robustness and strong generalization capability. This work presents an effective and scalable solution for intelligent endoscopic image analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Developing robust vision models requires large amounts of annotated data, especially for tasks involving fine-grained class distinctions under variable environmental conditions. Even in domains with numerous existing datasets, differences in annotation formats, label definitions and targeted learning tasks hinder data reuse and integration. To address this challenge, we introduce ReLEAP, a scalable Python framework for unifying heterogeneous image datasets through data-source abstraction, systematic reuse of annotations across learning tasks, and mapping of labels to a shared hierarchical taxonomy. We demonstrate its applicability in precision agriculture by integrating fifteen diverse datasets covering a broad range of plant species and annotation types. Beyond unification, ReLEAP enables systematic recycling of existing data and provides a robust foundation for learning-based annotation upcycling. Leveraging this foundation, we conduct extensive cross-dataset and fine-tuning experiments to investigate how domain-specific models trained on unified data and foundation models can be used to automatically generate higher-level annotations under unsupervised, weakly supervised and semi-supervised settings, and how dataset characteristics influence their effectiveness. Our results show that unified data access and annotation upcycling substantially expand the pool of reusable training data and annotations while improving data transparency, enabling more sustainable and resource-efficient use of existing datasets. We release the framework and trained models for academic use to support accessible and collaborative research, particularly in data-scarce and heterogeneous domains.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Self-supervised Vision Transformers (ViTs) like DINO encode rich semantics, enabling training-free segmentation of objects from the background and discovery of their parts via feature clustering. This paper focuses on part discovery: partitioning a foreground object into semantic parts. Existing approaches produce imprecise part boundaries. We trace this to the rigid ViT patch grid used in training DINO, where patches straddling boundaries contain pixels from multiple parts. While DINO's invariance-based training could absorb this pixel-level mixing into clean features, experiments with over 26K foreground patches show it does not: boundary-overlapping patches produce features with higher entropy and lower assignment confidence, blurring predicted part boundaries. Rather than make the massive effort of retraining DINO over geometrically coherent regions, an easier approach is to take boundaries directly from a boundary detector, which supplies the geometric information DINO's features lack. Motivated by this, we introduce Part-DINO, a training-free framework that combines boundaries from low-level image segmentation with DINO's semantic features. We use the boundaries to partition the image into regions, pool DINO features within each region, and group them to discover parts. Without any training, Part-DINO produces structurally coherent part assignments, outperforming training-free baselines across four standard benchmarks (CUB, CelebA, PartImageNet-OOD, PartImageNet-Seg) and remaining competitive with weakly-supervised and unsupervised methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Geographic regions are multimodal: satellite imagery captures visual morphology, text conveys human-interpretable semantics, and map structures describe urban context. Existing geospatial contrastive methods typically align image-text or image-location pairs, underusing structured map data. We introduce GeoLEON-CLIP, a graph-anchored contrastive framework that aligns three views of each H3-indexed region: an OpenStreetMap-derived graph embedding, a satellite image crop, and a textual description. Frozen pretrained LEON graph embeddings provide a semantic anchor between vision and language. Frozen graph, image, and text encoders are connected by trainable residual projection heads and trained with a symmetric tri-modal contrastive objective. Experiments show strong graph-image and graph-text alignment; graph-image retrieval achieves the best cross-modal performance in a mixed-resolution pool. To evaluate the anchoring hypothesis, we propose chained cross-modal recall $\mathrm{R}@A@B$, which routes retrieval through the graph modality and compares it with direct retrieval under a matched candidate budget. At $\mathrm{R}@1@1$, image$\rightarrow$graph$\rightarrow$text and text$\rightarrow$graph$\rightarrow$image outperform direct image-text retrieval by 4.8 and 4.4 points, respectively, despite using a single intermediate cell. Downstream probes show task-dependent gains, with the largest improvements on Airbnb and Beijing housing. These results indicate that OpenStreetMap graph structure can anchor multimodal representations combining maps, imagery, and language.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video moment retrieval aims to localize a query-relevant temporal segment in an untrimmed video. Existing methods typically perform video-text alignment in Euclidean space, where representations are embedded in a flat geometry. However, accurate moment localization requires organizing query-relevant evidence across multiple grounding states, where target regions, boundary transitions, and contextual distractors exhibit distinct temporal structures and relevance patterns. Such Euclidean representations lack an explicit geometry to jointly capture these relationships, motivating us to explore structured temporal evidence organization from a geometric perspective. Specifically, we propose Hyperbolic Temporal Evidence Organization (HTEO), a geometric framework that organizes query-aware temporal memory in hyperbolic space. HTEO maps temporal representations into a Poincaré ball and organizes temporal evidence through Radial Evidence Organization (REO) and Orthogonal Angular Refinement (OAR). REO adjusts temporal tokens' radial positions to encode the structural differences among temporal evidence, while OAR enhances query-conditioned semantic alignment through orthogonal rotation. The organized representation is then projected back to the Euclidean tangent space and injected into the decoder through a lightweight residual memory update, enabling hyperbolic geometric cues to be incorporated into standard Transformer decoders while preserving the original cross-modal memory. Furthermore, we introduce radial structure regularization and angular relevance supervision to explicitly guide temporal evidence organization and query-conditioned semantic alignment, respectively. Extensive experiments and analyses across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that HTEO improves temporal localization while providing an interpretable geometric organization of temporal evidence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Point-supervised Temporal Action Localization (PTAL) aims to localize action instances using only a single annotated timestamp for each instance. Such sparse supervision offers limited guidance for modeling the structural organization of an action instance. Existing PTAL methods primarily infer action regions from classification responses, leaving the structural organization of multiple action-related parts within each instance insufficiently modeled. To address this problem, we propose DeformStruct, an instance-level deformable structure modeling framework for PTAL. DeformStruct progressively constructs the structure of each action instance through three stages. First, Structural Anchor Mining discovers multiple anchors from annotated points and action-responsive regions, providing structural nodes grounded in the current instance. Second, Deformable Part Alignment associates latent structural parts with these anchors through deformation-aware matching, allowing their temporal supports to adapt to instance-dependent variations. Third, Relational Structural Organization explicitly models the relative temporal relations among the aligned parts, forming a relational representation that characterizes how different action-related parts are organized together. Extensive experiments on THUMOS14, ActivityNet-v1.3, GTEA, and BEOID demonstrate consistent improvements in point-supervised temporal localization and validate the effectiveness of deformable structural modeling.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Humans efficiently process multimodal streams spanning hours to years, building persistent, fine-grained spatiotemporal memories that support reasoning about and predicting long-horizon events. In contrast, existing benchmarks focus on short- and medium-context tasks, and most foundation models struggle to preserve fine-grained information over long horizons. To evaluate and improve long-horizon spatiotemporal reasoning, we introduce the LongVideo dataset and the 4DMemoryBench. LongVideo comprises about 900 hours of video and over 5,000 manually reviewed questions covering visual, textual, and spatiotemporal reasoning across diverse context lengths. We further develop the 4DMemory Harness, which integrates four complementary skills, 22+ specialized tool models, and a model-specific retrieval-augmented generation system. Evaluating 13 leading models, we find that standalone foundation models struggle with long-horizon tasks, while the 4DMemory Harness improves all of them by over 50\%. Compared to previous benchmark, the same models exhibit a performance drop of more than 10\% on 4DMemoryBench. We hope 4DMemory will facilitate the development of spatiotemporal agents capable of addressing complex, long-horizon tasks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) process images and text jointly, yet robustness evaluations often perturb these inputs separately or assess only predefined corruption levels. Such evaluations provide limited evidence of which modality is more susceptible to perturbations and whether smaller changes across both modalities can jointly expose failures. We present MoST, a black-box testing framework that characterizes modality sensitivity by comparing image-only, text-only, and joint perturbations. MoST uses multi-objective search to select and combine perturbations and adjust their severity, seeking prediction degradation while limiting changes to images and prompts. We evaluate MoST on four VLMs performing object localization on ImageNet and driving images. The models exhibit distinct sensitivities: textual perturbations dominate for some, whereas others are susceptible across both modalities. In several configurations, joint perturbations match or exceed the effectiveness of single-modality perturbations with smaller modifications to each input. In a human study, 60-76% of assessments across models judge the perturbed inputs sufficiently understandable to perform the task, and human localization is more accurate than that of the corresponding VLMs. These results show that evaluating modalities separately can overlook failure-inducing combinations and that modality sensitivity provides a more detailed account of VLM robustness than aggregate failure rates alone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Continuous Piecewise-Affine Based (CPAB) transformations, introduced by Freifeld et al. (2015, 2017), are flows obtained by integrating continuous piecewise-affine (CPA) velocity fields. These expressive transformations have proven useful in applications including nonlinear temporal warping and trainable activation functions in 1D, as well as nonrigid spatial deformations in 2D and 3D. While computational costs were not an issue in 1D, they originally limited the practical expressiveness of CPAB transformations in higher dimensions, especially in 3D. Our recent work (Aflalo et al., 2026) removed this bottleneck, enabling efficient optimization with much finer tessellations and substantially greater expressiveness. There is, however, a mathematical subtlety that was overlooked in the original CPAB papers (Freifeld et al., 2015, 2017). CPAB transformations are always bi-Lipschitz homeomorphisms, implying that both the transformations and their inverses are differentiable almost everywhere. They are not, however, necessarily diffeomorphisms. This note remedies the omission by identifying sufficient regularity conditions under which a CPAB transformation is guaranteed to be a diffeomorphism. We explain how differentiability can fail and how the proposed conditions prevent this failure. In 1D and 2D, we show that these conditions can be easily enforced and, for commonly used tessellations, hold with probability one under standard sampling assumptions. We expect analogous behavior in 3D.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Co-speech motion generation remains challenging over long horizons due to the need for temporal coherence and consistent style. Existing methods typically generate long motions segment by segment, either conditioning on a short seed of recent frames, which gradually drifts, or retaining ever-longer explicit histories, which are costly to maintain and hard to learn from limited data. We trace these limitations to a key asymmetry between past and present: past motion carries slowly varying style that should be compactly summarized, whereas the current segment exhibits fast-changing local dynamics that require fine-grained modeling. This asymmetry motivates a simple Past-As-State, Present-as-Attention principle for long-horizon co-speech motion generation. Following this principle, we propose PASPA, which summarizes past history with an inter-block persistent state and models the current segment with intra-block bidirectional self-attention. We instantiate PASPA within a blockwise flow matching framework, with multi-block supervision for effective training over variable histories, prefill-decode state caching for efficient rollout, and hybrid classifier-free guidance for enhanced history and speech conditioning. Extensive public-benchmark experiments show that PASPA achieves state-of-the-art FGD performance and preserves motion style across long-sequence generation.

</details>
