Abstract (英文): Accurate spectral and radiometric calibration is essential for reliable greenhouse gas retrieval from satellite hyperspectral observations. However, long-term in-orbit operation may introduce wavelength shifts and radiometric response inconsistencies, particularly within narrow atmospheric absorption bands, reducing the reliability of Level-1 radiance products. This study proposes an observation-dependent adaptive reference spectrum framework for spectral and radiometric calibration of the Greenhouse Gas Monitoring Instrument (GMI) onboard the GaoFen-5B (GF-5B) satellite. The proposed framework integrates the SCIATRAN radiative transfer model with the instrument line shape (ILS) of GMI to generate physically consistent reference spectra under varying observation conditions. Sensitivity analysis was performed to identify dominant parameters affecting simulated radiance spectra, and an adaptive reference spectrum library was constructed by considering surface reflectance, solar zenith angle, aerosol conditions, and geographic characteristics. The generated reference spectra were subsequently used to estimate spectral shifts and correct radiometric response deviations between observed and simulated spectra. GF-5B GMI observations acquired from September 2021 to December 2024 over Dunhuang, Golmud, and Railroad Valley (RRV) were used for validation. Results demonstrate that the proposed framework effectively reduces spectral offsets, improves radiometric consistency, and enhances spectral agreement for the O2, CO2-1, and CO2-2 bands. The multi-site validation confirms the robustness of the proposed approach under different surface and atmospheric conditions. This study provides a physically constrained and transferable calibration framework for improving the long-term spectral and radiometric consistency of greenhouse gas hyperspectral observations and supporting more reliable atmospheric composition retrievals.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Global climate change increases the frequency of synoptic forcing, across the Mediterranean Basin. These systems often trigger convective storms characterized by intense rainfall, resulting in the formation of linear erosion features (LEFs), such as gullies. Previous studies focused on the morphological characteristics of LEFs, whereas the relationships among LEF geomorphology, landscape topography, and rainfall were poorly studied. This knowledge gap is attributable to the multitude of topographic and rainfall variables, which make manual analysis both challenging and time-consuming. Recent advances in high-spatial-resolution satellite imagery, together with machine learning techniques, provide a potential alternative. Accordingly, the following objectives were defined: (1) design a framework for quantifying temporal changes in fresh herbaceous vegetation across different natural and human-induced linear erosion features (LEFs) in arid hilly loess landscapes using satellite imagery data; (2) develop a predictive model for geomorphological patterns of LEFs based on rainfall and topographic parameters. Three sites were studied, all in the northern Negev, with a semiarid climate and loess deposits covered with a dense LEF net. A unique framework was developed, including the use of acquired PlanetScope® imagery datasets, the allocation of elevation segments along the LEFs, and the normalization of the median NDVI value of each segment by that of the reference plot. Using ‘Random Forest’, the importance, and the directionality of the factors affecting the LEF fresh herbaceous over the elevation segments was determined by a linear correlation. The finding indicates that the NFVR of the LEF baseline (Intercept) was affected only by <1% from the aspect, while there was a 26% increase in NFVR downslope. The LEF (or segment) length affects the Intercept of the NFVR correlation, changing by 21% and 37%, respectively. The steepness affects the NFVR Intercept only by (0–8%), while there was a 43% effect on the NFVR change. In practice, the framework enables the prediction of segments vulnerable to land collapse, and adequate management of gullied areas.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Seabed pockmarks are important geomorphological indicators of shallow fluid migration, shallow gas activity, and gas hydrate-related processes. However, automatic pockmark detection from shaded-relief bathymetric imagery remains challenging because many pockmarks have small image-space footprints, occur densely, have weak boundaries, and are embedded in complex seabed backgrounds. To address these issues, this study proposes FSLA-RTDETR, an improved four-scale RT-DETR network for dense small-object seabed pockmark detection. The model introduces SAC-C2f to enhance high-level feature representation, the LPEA Encoder to improve global contextual modeling, and a DGCST-based bidirectional feature fusion network to integrate shallow spatial details and deep semantic information. In addition, the high-resolution P2 feature is incorporated into the decoder to improve small-object localization. A pockmark detection dataset was constructed from publicly available BOEM bathymetric data from the northern Gulf of Mexico, containing 880 images and 8013 annotated pockmark instances. Experimental results show that FSLA-RTDETR achieves mAP50, mAP75, and mAP50–95 values of 0.9218, 0.6221, and 0.5485, respectively. Compared with the baseline RT-DETR, mAP75 and mAP50–95 increase by 6.01 and 2.22 percentage points, respectively. Under the present no-pretraining setting, comparative experiments further demonstrate that FSLA-RTDETR achieves superior high-IoU localization performance compared with the representative object detection models evaluated in this study, providing an effective method for automatic seabed pockmark detection and geomorphological mapping.

Abstract (英文): The erosion of coastal chalk cliffs is a significant concern for communities in Northern Europe, with rates of retreat varying widely depending on local conditions. This study presents six years of UAV photogrammetry monitoring of chalk cliffs in Sussex, UK, producing 2.5D change detection maps to quantify erosion at Telscombe and Peacehaven. The results demonstrate a strong correlation between rates of lower-cliff erosion and wave energy at Peacehaven, with an r2 value of 0.82. However, periods of intensive mass-wasting can render these relationships unreliable. The relationship for Telscombe, which experiences much more mass-wasting, demonstrates an r2 of 0.37, which improves to 0.48 with the removal of an outlier. Cumulative erosion patterns follow a gradualistic trend, superimposed by episodic mass-wasting, with lower-cliff erosion rates increasing by approximately 3.2 times during storm-forced high-energy intervals compared to stable periods. A similar pattern is seen for mass-wasting events, although erosion intensities may be more than 6 times greater during periods of storminess. These findings highlight the role of storminess in accelerating erosion in the coastal zone.

Abstract (英文): Although deep learning-based approaches have significantly advanced intelligent radar image interpretation, existing methods predominantly rely on single-view inputs, which inherently limits target recognition performance. In practical scenarios, multi-view radar imagery provides richer structural and feature representations of targets compared to single-view observations. To effectively exploit this comprehensive information, we propose a new Multi-view Laplacian Propagation Fusion Network (MLPFN) for radar image target recognition. First, a Reparameterization Perspective-Aware (RPA) backbone is designed, incorporating a Dual-level Multi-path Rep-Kernel (DMPK) and a Multi-level Dual-path Attention Module (MDAM). This architecture mitigates the sensitivity of recognition performance to aspect angle variations and enhances the extraction of discriminative target features. Second, a Laplacian Multi-view Feature Propagation Algorithm (LMFPA) is introduced to encode structural correlations among different views, thereby augmenting the feature discriminability for multi-view targets. Third, a Multi-view Feature Adaptive Fusion Module (MFAFM) is developed to integrate global contextual information across views into a unified representation, reducing redundancy while maximizing feature distinctiveness. Furthermore, a Stage-wise Guided Constraint Training Mechanism (SGCTM) is devised to supervise the feature extraction process across different network layers during training, refining the network’s feature learning and recognition capabilities. Extensive experiments conducted on multi-view radar image datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed MLPFN.

</details>

The volumetric changes were assessed using DoD for the periods 2014–2020 and 2020–2024, and the morphometric performance of the TNO index was further validated using ROC curves in R. Both approaches performed consistently higher in detecting permanent gullies. The TNO index has proven its usefulness for semi-automated delimitation in large or low-data areas. By intersecting DoD surfaces with the gully inventory, localized erosion processes were successfully isolated from the surrounding agricultural landscape matrix. Abstract (英文): Mountain forest greenness varies across seasons, yet growing-season averages can mask season-specific relationships with climate variability. We assessed seasonal and elevational variation in historical forest greenness within areas classified as forest in 2024, temporal variation in spring NDVI–climate relationships, and the association between nighttime light (NTL) intensity and forest greenness in Changbai Mountain from 2000 to 2024. We used MODIS-derived normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI), 1 km temperature and precipitation datasets, elevation zones, and annual nighttime light (NTL) data. We applied a forest mask derived from the 2024 Annual China Land Cover Dataset (CLCD) and retained 1 km pixels with at least 70% forest cover. NDVI increased significantly over the aggregate growing season (slope = 0.0011 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.584, p < 0.001), summer (slope = 0.0006 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.188, p = 0.030), and autumn (slope = 0.0019 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.280, p = 0.007). Autumn showed the largest estimated slope; this ranking alone does not establish statistical differences among seasonal slopes. Spring showed no significant long-term trend (slope = 0.0011 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.087, p = 0.152), but spring NDVI showed a strong positive correlation with temperature at the regional scale (r = 0.760, p < 0.001), with positive correlations maintained across all elevation zones. In spring regression models, temperature showed a larger standardized coefficient magnitude than precipitation across elevation zones, with all variance inflation factors ≤ 1.22. Exploratory five-year sliding-window correlations suggested temporal variation in spring NDVI–climate relationships, although each estimate was based on only five annual observations. At low elevation, the NTL–NDVI regression was significant (R2 = 0.198, p = 0.026), whereas associations at middle and high elevations were not statistically significant; this raw association should be interpreted cautiously because both variables may contain concurrent temporal trends. These results describe seasonal and elevational greenness patterns within the 2024 forest mask, highlighting the distinct spring temperature association and elevation-dependent relationships between NTL intensity and forest greenness.

Abstract (英文): Timely and accurate mapping of flood inundation extents in rice-growing regions is essential for flood damage assessment. However, fragmented fields and heterogeneous land cover in complex terrain continue to pose challenges. This study proposes a two-stage mapping framework that integrates multi-resolution remote sensing imagery with deep-learning-based semantic segmentation. First, a dual-attention and edge-aware attention network (DAEA-Net) was developed to derive a high-accuracy paddy rice map from very high-resolution (VHR) imagery. Second, the flood extent in paddy fields was delineated using the prior-histogram marker-controlled watershed (PH-MCW) method on SAR imagery with area-of-interest (AOI) filtering based on terrain slope and on the permanent water bodies and potentially inundated land types of the land-type survey data. On the test set, DAEA-Net achieved the highest OA, IoU, F1-score, and Kappa among the compared semantic segmentation models, including U-Net, HRNet, and DeepLabV3+, with values of 0.9205, 0.8747, 0.9331, and 0.8352, respectively. PH-MCW segmentation with AOI filtering, which removes terrain-induced false alarms while retaining water surfaces, improved flood-water detection, achieving F1-scores of 0.821 and 0.764 on two flood-validation reference datasets (NDRCC and AIR-PolSAR-Seg-2.0), respectively. The proposed framework provides a semi-automated solution for mapping flood inundation over paddy rice fields in complex terrain, in which the prior water proportion is refined by local inspection before application.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marine gravity is traditionally derived from altimeter data using physics-based methods, which are sensitive to altimeter data noise. Although deep learning methods can capture nonlinear relationships between marine gravity and multi-source data, purely data-driven approaches depend strongly on the quality and spatial distribution of training ship-borne gravity data. To combine the advantages of both methods, this study proposes a hybrid marine gravity recovery method that integrates the Inverse Vening Meinesz (IVM) method into a convolutional neural network (CNN) framework. Specifically, the IVM-derived marine gravity is incorporated into the CNN loss function as a constraint, and distance- and topography-dependent hyperparameters are introduced to control its contribution during training. The gravity model derived by the proposed IVM-constrained CNN method is validated using independent ship-borne gravity observations and a recognized marine gravity model. Assessed by ship-borne data, the proposed method improves accuracy by 28.5% and 6.8% compared with the IVM-derived and CNN-derived gravity models, respectively. Comparisons with the recognized model also indicate that the gravity model derived by the proposed method achieves the highest accuracy among the three models. These results show that embedding IVM constraints into deep learning frameworks can significantly improve the accuracy and robustness of marine gravity recovery.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Lake water color provides an integrated indicator of aquatic environmental change, yet its long-term phenology and resilience remain poorly understood in arid regions. In this study, Landsat-derived hue angle (α) records were reconstructed for 350 lakes larger than 1 km2 across arid Northwest China from 1984 to 2025. Regional mean α declined significantly over the past 42 years (R2 = 0.51), accompanied by pronounced spatial heterogeneity. Three lake water color phenological types were identified: Aseasonal (75.0%), Summer Greening (13.7%), and Spring Greening (11.3%). Aseasonal lakes were the most stable, whereas seasonal lakes showed greater temporal variability and a tendency to transition toward aseasonality. Lake water color resilience increased in 43% of lakes, declined in 21%, and remained unchanged in 36%, with resilience-loss lakes showing distinct spatial clustering. Lake morphology was most strongly associated with water color change, topography and thermal conditions with its phenology, and watershed population dynamics with its resilience. In situ observations further showed significant relationships between α and optically active constituents and their derived parameters, reflecting the combined optical effects of absorption and scattering and suggesting its potential as a proxy for broader water-quality assessment with more extensive field sampling and validation. These findings highlight contrasting controls on lake-color dynamics, phenology, and resilience, and demonstrate the value of hue angle for monitoring environmental change in vulnerable arid-zone lakes. Overall, these results advance understanding of the long-term environmental responses of arid-zone lakes and provide a scientific basis for targeted monitoring and adaptive management in arid regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vendor-provided rational polynomial coefficients (RPCs) for high-resolution optical satellite imagery often contain systematic biases that are commonly corrected using field-surveyed ground control points (GCPs). Acquiring such points can be costly or infeasible in inaccessible regions. This study proposes an RPC bias compensation framework for KOMPSAT-3 (K3) optical imagery based on the geometric accuracy and stability of KOMPSAT-5 (K5) X-band SAR enhanced high-resolution (EH) imagery, without measuring image coordinates of control points. Three-dimensional ground control data (GCD) are projected into both sensor image spaces via the K5 Range–Doppler (RD) model and the K3 RPC model. A GCD-driven projective transformation provides coarse alignment, followed by fine registration using FFT-based phase correlation (FPC). Registration-derived correspondence coordinates are then used to estimate a strongly regularized image-space affine bias model, yielding a predominantly translational correction. Three GCD sources—Korean public control points (PCPs), ICESat-2 altimetry, and the 30 m SRTM DEM—were evaluated using one K3–K5 image pair over Suncheon, Korea. A sensitivity analysis showed that control points on flat terrain (elevation ≤60 m, slope ≤6°) yielded stable results, whereas points on steep or forested terrain degraded the registration, and increasing the number of GCD points did not systematically improve accuracy. Under these conditions, all three sources reduced the RMSE at independent GNSS checkpoints from 7.1 and 10.6 pixels in the column and row directions to 1.6–2.0 and 1.6–2.1 pixels, respectively. This accuracy is comparable to that of conventional GCP-based compensation (1.7/1.3 pixels), whereas two structural-feature-based matching methods (HOPC and CFOG) did not improve on the original RPCs under the tested implementations. These results demonstrate the potential of SRTM-derived control data for meter-level directional refinement of optical imagery when reliable SAR–optical registration is achieved. Generalization beyond the tested image pair requires further validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Weakly supervised remote sensing change detection reduces annotation costs but suffers from incomplete class activation maps, unreliable pseudo-label propagation, and insufficient discrimination between adjacent change regions. To address these limitations, we propose PRIWCD, a weakly supervised framework for remote sensing change detection. PRIWCD introduces a dynamic prototype comparison module to enhance high-level differential features by modeling unchanged regions and diverse change patterns with multiple dynamic prototypes. A dual relationship propagation guidance module further refines class activation maps by jointly exploiting changes in bi-temporal attention relationships and feature similarity. In addition, instance separation regularization improves feature compactness within pseudo-instances and enhances the separation between changed regions and the background. Experiments on WHU-CD, CLCD, and DSIFN demonstrate the effectiveness of PRIWCD. The proposed method achieves the best mean F1 scores of 79.10%, 62.33%, and 62.68%, respectively, outperforming the compared weakly supervised methods. The results demonstrate that PRIWCD effectively improves pseudo-label quality and provides robust change detection under image-level supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To address the challenge of accurately inverting cloud base height (CBH) from passive remote sensing, this study proposes Hetero-CBHNet, a heterogeneous stacked integrated network framework for CBH inversion. The framework uses random forest (RF), convolutional neural network (CNN), and Transformer models as a pool of candidate base models and employs the RidgeCV algorithm to automatically learn the fusion weights. A total of 1,011,620 samples from FY-4A and MODIS, covering the period from May 2019 to May 2020, were selected (with CloudSat/CALIPSO 2B-CLDCLASS-LIDAR as the ground truth), and the dataset was split chronologically. Across the two data sources under the chronological splitting protocol, Hetero-CBHNet achieves RMSE values of 2966.12 m and 2797.64 m and R values of 0.630 and 0.659 on the test sets, outperforming the individual models. Feature analysis shows that incorporating operational passive cloud type products does not yield a stable gain, indicating that current passive cloud type information is not yet sufficient to replace active satellite labels. In addition, the RMSE values under the random splitting protocol are 1510.01 m and 1488.41 m, substantially better than those under chronological splitting, which confirms the strong dependence of the conclusions on the evaluation protocol.

Abstract (英文): Long-term satellite precipitable water vapor (PWV) records are widely used in climate studies, but excellent monthly agreement does not necessarily imply equivalent long-term climate-record consistency. We evaluated Atmospheric Infrared Sounder (AIRS) Version 7 Level-3 monthly PWV against Integrated Global Radiosonde Archive Version 2 (IGRA2) observations at 76 Chinese stations during 2005–2025, using 19,053 matched station-months. The overall agreement was high (R = 0.984, bias = −1.34 mm, RMSE = 3.36 mm). Nevertheless, station-by-calendar-month leave-one-year-out anomalies showed a robust moisture-state dependence of AIRS–IGRA differences (Spearman ρ = −0.722; 95% CI −0.741 to −0.704). The AIRS-on-IGRA OLS anomaly-response slope was 0.473; equal-variance Deming regression increased it to 0.560 (95% CI 0.519–0.596), still below unity. The ascending-minus-descending monthly PWV averaged +0.561 mm and was largest in summer. The network annual bias became more negative at −0.141 mm yr−1, compared with −0.138 mm yr−1 for a strict 73-station fixed network, while the annual R had no significant trend. The station-scale trend consistency was moderate (Pearson r = 0.545; sign agreement 65.8%). An independent BJFS GNSS comparison supported an AIRS contribution to local divergence, but did not establish nationwide attribution. These findings support monthly PWV climatology and broad variability applications over China while showing that small long-term trends require independent constraints, reference-record homogenization, and explicit attention to sampling stability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Nighttime light (NTL) remote sensing provides a globally consistent means of observation of human activity after dark, but its sustainability meaning is context dependent. This study analyzes annual VIIRS Day/Night Band nighttime-light radiance for 467 urban centers from 2015 to 2023 using the same 2015 Global Human Settlement Layer urban-center boundary for every year. Holding the spatial footprint constant provides an apples-to-apples longitudinal comparison: changes in Sum VNL represent changes in satellite-observed radiance within the same geographic area rather than changes caused by expanding or redefining the urban boundary. Across the 467 fixed urban areas, total Sum VNL increased from 7.43 million in 2015 to 8.51 million in 2023 (+14.49%); 374 cities increased and 93 decreased. The sample declined by 2.49% from 2019 to 2020 and increased by 5.44% from 2020 to 2021. Regional 2015–2023 changes were +27.96% in Asia, +16.17% in Africa, +14.78% in Oceania, +11.69% in Latin America and the Caribbean, +3.73% in Northern America, and −1.96% in Europe. Mean VNL is reported alongside Sum VNL to distinguish average radiance intensity from total radiance within each fixed urban footprint. The results demonstrate substantial spatial and temporal heterogeneity but do not by themselves prove energy poverty, over-illumination, electricity consumption, or health exposure. NTL is therefore interpreted as a complementary diagnostic variable for SDGs 3, 7, and 11 that requires contextual validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-resolution multi-timescale SPEI data are important for drought monitoring and climate-impact studies, but long-term high-resolution datasets remain limited over large regions. This study developed and technically validated a station-supervised monthly multi-timescale SPEI dataset for mainland China from 1979 to 2018 on a 1 km grid, including SPEI-1, SPEI-3, SPEI-6, SPEI-12, and SPEI-24. Monthly labels were extracted from daily records at 427 stations using an exact source-calendar month-end rule. Monthly 0.1° CMFD predictors were mapped to stations and 1 km pixel centres using a NaN-aware bilinear sampler and combined with complete-window temporal features and NASADEM terrain covariates. Five timescale-specific LightGBM models generated 2359 nationwide monthly rasters. Random five-fold cross-validation yielded mean R2 values of 0.8807–0.9122; 200 km spatial-block validation with a 100 km exclusion buffer yielded 0.7857–0.8739; and forward blocked-period validation yielded 0.7577–0.8337. Full-period product comparison gave Pearson r = 0.9295 against Zhang-SPEI for SPEI-3, while comparison with HSPEI across five timescales yielded r = 0.7772–0.3267. Spatially buffered drought-condition validation produced F1 scores of 0.7021–0.7838 for moderate-or-worse drought, whereas extreme-drought Recall was 0.2397–0.3869. The dataset provides a long-term, multi-timescale resource for drought monitoring and climate-impact research across mainland China, with validation results characterizing its performance across regions, periods, and drought-severity levels.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Aerosol optical depth characterizes total aerosol loading in the atmospheric column but does not directly indicate its vertical distribution relative to the planetary boundary layer. This study investigates aerosol vertical structure over Daejeon, Korea, by integrating column aerosol optical properties from a sky radiometer, near-surface atmospheric extinction measurements, and planetary boundary layer height from reanalysis data. Aerosol scale height is derived from aerosol optical depth at 870 nm and near-surface extinction and is analyzed alongside aerosol optical and microphysical properties. Aerosol scale height exhibits substantial variability relative to the planetary boundary layer height, with the highest relative values occurring in spring and the lowest in autumn. An increase in aerosol scale height relative to the boundary layer is accompanied by a decrease in the Ångström exponent and an increase in the coarse-mode volume fraction. The coarse-mode enhancement is driven mainly by an increase in coarse-mode aerosol volume, whereas surface PM10 concentrations show only a weak relationship with variations in vertical structure. Single scattering albedo generally increases, indicating a more scattering dominated column aerosol population. These results demonstrate that combining aerosol scale height with planetary boundary layer height provides valuable information on aerosol vertical structure and the degree of correspondence between surface and column aerosol conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sea ice detection and classification are critical for navigation safety and climate change research. With its high-resolution, all-weather, and day-and-night imaging capabilities, Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) has become a vital data source for these tasks. However, most existing deep learning studies rely on amplitude data, failing to fully exploit complex-valued information. This study evaluates whether locally filtered inter-channel phase information from Sentinel-1 Single Look Complex (SLC) data provides complementary information to conventional HH and HV amplitudes. Using the AI4 Arctic Sea Ice Challenge Dataset (ASID Challenge), we construct a complex-valued dataset and evaluate different input combinations with four representative semantic segmentation networks. We examine the classification results across six different categories in the ASID Challenge and, in conjunction with real navigation scenarios, map these six categories into three classes: Open Water, Early-stage Ice, and Developed Ice. For FCN under direct three-class training, phase augmentation improved the mean test Macro-F1 and mIoU by 4.4% and 6.1%. The results show that although its benefit is not consistent across all six-class classification experiments, phase information provides more reliable improvements when sea ice types are grouped according to broader development stages. These findings suggest that inter-channel phase contains complementary information beyond amplitude and may improve SAR sea ice classification under practical application scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spinach (Spinacia oleracea L.) is an economically important leafy vegetable whose nitrogen management remains comparatively understudied with remote sensing tools relative to major row crops. This study evaluated the potential of unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-based hyperspectral imagery to estimate foliar nitrogen content (%N) in field-grown spinach at a commercial plot across two growing seasons and four nitrogen fertilization treatments. Nine spectral indices were related to destructively measured %N at four phenological stages of the Biologische Bundesanstalt, Bundessortenamt und CHemische Industrie (BBCH) scale (BBCH 16, 30, 45, and 49) under three modeling scenarios, ranging from flight-specific regression to a continuous model incorporating days after sowing (DAS) as a proxy for crop development, with model performance assessed out-of-sample by repeated cross-validation. Foliar nitrogen content differed significantly among treatments from BBCH 30 onward and only marginally at the earliest stage, with the two highest doses converging into a statistically indistinguishable class. The Vogelmann red-edge index 3 (VOGREI3) yielded the most accurate estimates, peaking at BBCH 45 (cross-validated coefficient of determination, R2 = 0.845) before declining significantly at BBCH 49, a loss consistent with spectral saturation of the closed canopy. A single continuous model incorporating DAS covered the BBCH 45 and BBCH 49 flights with a relative error of 12.4%, against 9.6% and 14.9% for the corresponding flight-specific models, and required no stage-specific recalibration within the calibration seasons. In an unseen season, plot ranking was preserved, and a few reference samples corrected the bias. UAV hyperspectral imagery combined with development-stage-aware modeling therefore shows potential for non-destructive nitrogen monitoring in spinach once locally calibrated, although transferability across further seasons, sites and cultivars remains to be established.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Terrain generalization—the scale-driven abstraction of digital elevation models (DEMs) that retains dominant landforms while filtering trivial microtopography—has long been dominated by rule-based algorithms, whereas deep learning has mainly targeted the inverse task of DEM super-resolution. This study proposes TG-CNN, a feature-fusion convolutional neural network for DEM down-scaling generalization. The network compresses the high-resolution DEM through a max- or average-pooling front end, combines normalized elevation with slope and sine–cosine circularly encoded aspect as complementary input channels, and is trained with a composite elevation–slope–aspect loss. Training, validation, and testing are conducted on geographically independent mountainous areas under a leave-one-area-out scheme, followed by adjustable IDW fusion post-processing. On the test region, classical bilinear interpolation retains the best elevation accuracy, while the standalone TG-CNN does not reach a comparable level; fused accuracy improves monotonically as the IDW share increases, indicating that the fusion benefit originates from the interpolation component. On the ridge–valley structure, however, TG-CNN behaves as an aggressive yet reliable filter: it achieves the highest precision and the smallest positional offset among all compared methods but retains only part of the reference skeleton, leaving its F1-score below the interpolation baseline. TG-CNN thus realizes a genuine, controllable terrain-abstraction effect, yet deep learning does not yet surpass mature interpolation in pixel-level elevation accuracy—a negative result that clarifies current limitations and points toward residual learning and information-preserving architectures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monocular height estimation plays a critical role in 3D perception for remote sensing, offering a cost-effective alternative to multi-view or LiDAR-based methods. While deep learning has significantly advanced the capabilities of monocular height estimation, these methods remain fundamentally limited by the availability of labeled data, which are expensive and labor-intensive to obtain at scale. The scarcity of high-quality annotations hinders the generalization and performance of existing models. To overcome this limitation, we propose leveraging large volumes of unlabeled data through a semi-supervised learning framework, enabling the model to extract informative cues from unlabeled samples and improve its predictive performance. In this work, we introduce Teacher-Student-Exam Network (TSE-Net), a self-training pipeline for semi-supervised monocular height estimation. The pipeline integrates teacher, student, and exam networks. The student network is trained on unlabeled data using pseudo-labels generated by the teacher network, while the exam network functions as a temporal ensemble of the student network to stabilize performance. The teacher network is formulated as a joint regression and classification model: the regression branch predicts height values that serve as pseudo-labels, and the classification branch predicts height value classes along with class probabilities, which are used to filter pseudo-labels. Height value classes are defined using a novel Hierarchical Bi-Cut (HBC) strategy to address the inherent long-tailed distribution of heights, and the predicted class probabilities are calibrated with a Plackett-Luce model to reflect the expected accuracy of pseudo-labels. We evaluate the proposed pipeline on three datasets spanning different resolutions and imaging modalities: the ISPRS Vaihingen dataset (IR-R-G aerial images, 0.09 m resolution), the SynRS3D dataset (synthetic images, 0.6–1 m resolution), and the GBH dataset (satellite RGB images, 3 m resolution). Experimental results demonstrate that our approach outperforms supervised and semi-supervised baselines in low-label regimes, and ablation studies confirm the contribution and dataset-dependent behavior of each design component. Code is available at https://github.com/zhu-xlab/tse-net . • First self-training-based semi-supervised learning pipeline for monocular height estimation. • Teacher-Student-Exam pipeline with classification-regression teacher. • Hierarchical Bi-Cut reduces long-tailed biases in height predictions. • Plackett-Luce model aligns pseudo-label reliability with class confidence. • Up to 29.07% gap reduction between 100% and 0.1% labeled data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Optical remote sensing imagery is frequently degraded by cloud and cloud-shadow contamination, which limits its reliability for near-real-time land use and land cover (LULC) mapping. Although synthetic aperture radar (SAR) can provide cloud-penetrating structural information, existing SAR-optical fusion methods often assume reliable optical observations and insufficiently address the semantic uncertainty introduced by cloud contamination. To address this issue, we propose CloudLULC-Net, an end-to-end heterogeneous SAR-optical fusion framework that directly predicts LULC maps from cloud-contaminated Sentinel-2 imagery and temporally adjacent Sentinel-1 SAR observations. The proposed network incorporates optical reliability modulation to suppress unreliable optical responses, heterogeneous information adaptive aggregation to model high-order spatial-channel interactions between optical and SAR representations, and a unified semantic mapping transformer to organize fused features in a LULC-oriented latent space. A semantic anchor-guided optimization strategy is further introduced to improve the consistency of intermediate semantic representations. To support this task, we construct CloudLULC-Set, a large-scale benchmark dataset containing 40,223 curated SAR-optical-label triplets with pixel-level LULC annotations across diverse geographic regions and cloud conditions. Experimental results show that CloudLULC-Net achieves an OA of 86.60%, an F1-score of 83.29%, and an mIoU of 73.51%, outperforming representative heterogeneous reconstruction-first and end-to-end SAR-optical mapping methods. Comparisons with existing global LULC products and analyses under different cloud-cover levels further demonstrate the robustness and practical value of CloudLULC-Net for target-date LULC mapping in cloud-prone regions.The project is publicly available at: https://github.com/RSIIPAC/CloudLULC

To bridge this gap, we introduce \dataset, a spoken-query benchmark derived from RISBench that adds accent- and voice-diverse speech while preserving the original image, mask, and data splits. Its hard evaluation sets combine rotor, wind, and mixed interference with three signal-to-noise levels. We also propose \model, an efficient bilateral network that combines a boundary-preserving visual path with token-preserving speech encoding, kernel linear cross-modal attention, and a resolution refinement head. The design conditions visual features at two scales without materializing a dense speech--visual affinity matrix, then restores fine boundaries using high-resolution visual features. On the clean test split, \model with Swin-Base achieves 62.09\% mean intersection over union (mIoU) and 68.22\% overall intersection over union (oIoU), outperforming the strongest audio-adapted remote-sensing baseline by 5.38 and 2.08 percentage points, respectively. It retains the best hard-set mIoU at 54.09\%. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first benchmark and model study of full-sentence spoken-query referring segmentation for remote-sensing imagery. The code will be made publicly available.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Limited labeled data remains a central challenge for deep-learning-based sea-ice type mapping, while cross-channel relationships remain underexploited, and interaction mechanisms are lacking. This study proposes ESICM, combining Sentinel-1 SAR, AMSR2 brightness temperatures, and ERA5 environmental variables for pixel-level mapping of open water (OW), young ice (YI), first-year ice (FYI), and multi-year ice (MYI). ESICM adopts prototype-based metric learning to fully exploit labels, incorporates a channel multiplication-division module to enhance cross-channel interactions, and introduces two interaction functions, supports-input selection and user dense prompts, to improve classification in hard-to-classify scenes. On the AI4Arctic dataset, trained on 492 AI4Arctic scenes and tested on 20 scenes, ESICM achieved 93.34% overall accuracy, 93.08% F1 score (F1), 88.40% Kappa coefficient, and 77.29% mean intersection over union, exceeding the second-best results by 0.85%–1.55%. Under challenging melt-affected conditions, MYI F1 and intersection over union exceeded the second-best results by 3.13% and 3.93%. Across forward-temporal, cross-region, reduced-training-data, and cross-dataset experiments, ESICM ranked first for 14 and second for two of 16 metrics, indicating relatively strong generalization. In two hard-to-classify scenes, simulated user prompts covering 10% of valid pixels improved all four metrics by more than 4% and 20% over prompt-free inference, respectively, supporting the technical feasibility of interaction. SHAP attributions were broadly consistent with established sea-ice remote-sensing knowledge, enhancing the model’s post hoc interpretability. Overall, ESICM improved sea-ice mapping with limited labeled data and showed application potential under controlled distribution shifts and simulated interaction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Land surface temperature (LST) is a critical parameter for characterizing surface energy balance and hydrothermal processes. While Landsat provides invaluable LST observations at medium spatial resolution for over 40 years, its native spatial resolution of thermal bands (e.g., 100 m) remains insufficient compared to its 30 m optical bands, failing to meet the demands of fine-scale studies. To address this issues, this study proposes a progressive self-training framework for downscaling Landsat LST to 30 m without relying on fine-scale ground truth, while maintaining minimal data dependence. The framework progressively optimizes a cross-modal fusion network to refine thermal details in a coarse-to-fine manner, characterized by one pre-training and two fine-tuning stages. Spatial validation against SDGSAT-1 30 m LST and temporal validation using in situ measurements confirm its reliability and accuracy, with both station-averaged MAE and RMSE outperforming the official cubic product by approximately 0.4 K. Further performance comparison experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework consistently reconstructs coherent fine-scale thermal patterns while preserving spatial heterogeneity. Multi spatial resolution evaluations and ablation studies verify the effectiveness of the proposed strategy and network design. Overall, the framework provides a stable pathway for enhancing the spatial resolution of Landsat LST, providing fine-resolution data support for fine-scale surface process studies and localized environmental monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Living in a changing climate, human society now faces more frequent and severe natural disasters than ever before. As a consequence, rapid disaster response during the "Golden 72 Hours" of search and rescue becomes a vital humanitarian necessity and community concern. However, traditional disaster damage surveys routinely fail to generalize across distinct urban morphologies and new disaster events. Effective damage mapping typically requires exhaustive and time-consuming manual data annotation. To address this issue, we introduce Smart Transfer, a novel Geospatial Artificial Intelligence (GeoAI) framework, leveraging state-of-the-art vision Foundation Models (FMs) for rapid building damage mapping with post-earthquake Very High Resolution (VHR) imagery. Specifically, we design two novel model transfer strategies: first, Pixel-wise Clustering (PC), ensuring robust prototype-level global feature alignment; second, a Distance-Penalized Triplet (DPT), integrating patch-level spatial autocorrelation patterns by assigning stronger penalties to semantically inconsistent yet spatially adjacent patches. Extensive experiments and ablations from the recent 2023 Turkiye-Syria earthquake show promising performance in multiple cross-region transfer settings, namely Leave One Domain Out (LODO) and Specific Source Domain Combination (SSDC). Moreover, Smart Transfer provides a scalable, automated GeoAI solution to accelerate building damage mapping and support rapid disaster response, offering new opportunities to enhance disaster resilience in climate-vulnerable regions and communities. The data and code are publicly available at https://github.com/ai4city-hkust/SmartTransfer.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Enterprise customer journeys are shaped not only by product experiences and marketing, but also by recurring resource-allocation decisions. In B2B go-to-market (GTM) organizations, territory carving determines which accounts receive seller coverage, which seller-account relationships are preserved, and how limited field capacity is distributed. Yet the process often remains manual, making it difficult to compare opportunities, value continuity, and evaluate tradeoffs among growth, balance, capacity, and feasibility.
  We present a production decision system for tradeoff-aware customer-to-seller allocation in enterprise GTM planning. It integrates three components: a multidimensional account-value layer that combines predictive signals to score opportunity; an observational continuity-valuation layer that estimates the cost of breaking seller-account relationships using a synthetic-control-style counterfactual framework; and a multi-objective optimization layer that allocates coverage to high-value accounts while considering continuity, opportunity balance, and operational constraints.
  Deployed as a scenario-based decision-support system, the framework enables planners to inspect and compare allocation tradeoffs. Historical backtesting shows that optimizer-generated scenarios substantially improve assignment compliance while maintaining comparable seller-level opportunity distribution. In production planning, adopted scenarios improved both assignment compliance and opportunity balance relative to the previous baseline. Directing scarce seller capacity toward higher-value accounts also offers incremental revenue potential at enterprise scale. The key contribution is an integrated, inspectable framework that turns territory carving into a governable decision process.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Scientific computing increasingly relies on large language model-based agents for performance engineering tasks such as instrumentation, analysis, and workflow porting. However, agent effectiveness depends heavily on the surrounding harness, which typically relies on manually written skills and curated memory. This limits autonomous learning, forcing agents to repeatedly rediscover solutions rather than retain and reuse knowledge from prior executions. We introduce Phronix, a capability-centric learning paradigm that enables agentic harnesses to acquire, validate, and refine reusable capabilities from their own executions. Phronix operates through a predictor-validator-corrector loop: MCP tools and associated skills propose solutions, tool executions validate them, and observed outcomes refine the stored capabilities. It distills every observed interaction into a capability store and researches a failing task rather than abandoning it. First, such learned capabilities solve a recurring task on the first attempt in 93-95% of cases across every model, significantly reducing solution non-determinism and improving reliability. Second, Phronix turns an agent's own execution into 7.6-30.8 durable capabilities per task without user instruction, and creation falls below two once the needed capability exists. Third, warm-start runs consume only 0.38-0.40 million tokens per task, which is three to four times less than a single baseline attempt, while a cold-start costs no more than the baseline. On the 1000 Genome Pegasus workflow, instrumenting a production application for I/O performance analysis, Phronix passes all 11 correctness checks against six for the baseline harness, and also lifts the task solving rates of two cheaper models from 29% and 27% of SWE-bench tasks solved to 91% and 87%, respectively, matching the performance of the most expensive evaluated model.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Patronizing and condescending language (PCL) is a form of harmful language that belittles and assumes pity for vulnerable groups, reinforcing negative societal stereotypes. However, unlike overt hate speech, it is often difficult to detect, even for human judges. This paper proposes a system for the automated detection of PCL. Our first approach uses static word embeddings and a convolutional neural network for classification, while our second approach uses pre-trained BERT embeddings fine-tuned for the PCL task. Our second approach was considerably more successful, and achieved an F1 score of 0.528 over the development set.

Abstract (英文): Even a carefully proofread paper can leave small gaps in its proofs or its stated assumptions. We introduce PaperFormalizer, a Lean-based agentic pipeline that helps authors find and close such gaps in their own papers and turn each paper into a Lean-verified version. Starting from the LaTeX source, PaperFormalizer transcribes the paper into a dependency graph of statements, audits each statement in natural language for faithfulness, ambiguity and correctness, and then formalizes and proves the statements in Lean against Mathlib, optional domain libraries and the author's personal toolkit. Only the author knows what a statement is meant to say and how a formalization gap in a result is best fixed. The pipeline therefore stops and asks the author whenever a statement admits more than one reading, presents every repair, in natural language and TeX symbols, as a proposal that the author can accept, decline or redirect, and leaves to the author every decision that could potentially change what the paper claims. The output is an annotated and revised version of the paper, a report of the changes, and Lean code in which each declaration is linked to the statement it formalizes. The author's toolkit grows as more papers are formalized, and authors can share their verified papers so that researchers who build on them can reuse their Lean code. By making verified papers practical to produce, PaperFormalizer aims to make research that rests on mathematical proofs more robust. PaperFormalizer runs inside a coding agent (Claude Code or Codex) and is available at https://github.com/Jiyuan-Tan/PaperFormalizer. Papers with Lean code shared by users are available at https://paperformalizer.org.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Intelligent agents should learn from their mistakes so that experience leads to continuous improvement. Yet today's agents recover from a mistake only temporarily: in-context reflections are easily forgotten across sessions and compete for limited context, while full-parameter fine-tuning is expensive and can interfere with existing capabilities. We introduce Self-Direction, a mechanism for agents to learn from mistakes that combines the benefits of temporary in-context retries and policy updates. When an agent fails a task, it reflects on its own trajectory in order to extract a lesson and immediately distills it into a lightweight low-rank adapter. By keeping the original weights frozen, Self-Direction prevents adapter training from overwriting base-model parameters while training additional parameters equivalent to ~0.02% of the base model's parameter count for each adapter. The agent then saves these adapters in a persistent library of lessons, automatically routing them to solve related problems later. Experiments with agents based on open-weight models from the Qwen family across open-ended research (GAIA), tool-use workflows (Claw-Eval), and terminal tasks (Terminal-Bench 2.1) show that our method improves overall success rates by 8.9 to 17.0 percentage points and performs competitively with frontier systems. We find that Self-Direction reduces repeated mistakes across future encounters of a task, nearly doubling the reliability of an agent's corrections. Learned lessons also transfer: a handful of adapters generalize to dozens of new tasks without additional training. Ultimately, this self-directed repair transforms everyday mistakes into lasting lessons.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present the framework of TACS-RF - a Tool-driven Agentic Circuit Synthesis of RF and mmWave modules. In this framework, a large-language-model (LLM) coding agent (Claude Opus 4.8) directly drives industry-standard electronic-design-automation (EDA) tools such as Cadence Virtuoso/Spectre, Calibre, Cadence EMX, and Ansys HFSS to design RF and mmWave modules. We validate the framework through the agentic design of a 240-251 GHz sub-THz transmitter in a 90 nm SiGe BiCMOS process. The agent semi-autonomously designed the sub-blocks including a 60 GHz push-push Colpitts VCO with second-harmonic extraction, multistage differential cascode power drivers, a push-push frequency doubler, a differential bowtie antenna, and all the interstage matching networks. The transmitter chain with parasitic-extracted devices and EM-verified passives delivers +5.1 to +6.3 dBm across an 11 GHz varactor-only tuning range, phase noise of -80 to -88 dBc/Hz at 1 MHz offset while consuming a DC power of 361 mW. We describe the agent-driven design flow and the resulting division of labor between human and agent, taking a step toward a "text-to-ASIC" future for designing RF/mmWave hardware.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A durable agent can resume a consequential action while its authority changes and the action's external outcome remains uncertain. We study execution-time reliability of tool-using AI agents, isolating recovery decisions from language-model behavior. Checkpoint state, permission to dispatch, evidence of an external effect and permission to reconcile are distinct obligations. We present a finite conformance study that checks these obligations through histories scored against a provider effect ledger hidden from the recovering application. A prospectively frozen campaign executes 336 histories on native LangGraph and Temporal hosts, crossing 14 schedules, three provider contracts and four matched application policies. All 168 paired host cells have identical checked outcomes. Per host, retained-ledger reconciliation passes all 42 histories: 21 checked completions, 15 denials and six unknown outcomes with reservations retained. Cached retry, revalidation before retry and stable-key retry pass 30, 36 and 31 histories respectively. A separate 16-case component study of InfinityOS, an enterprise AI platform developed by Arya.ai, exercises exported authority, worker, recovery and settlement code over repository fakes. The results isolate the roles of current authority, provider contracts and retained observations in recovery, with safety, completion and unresolved obligations reported separately for the declared application profile.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Distilling interactive LLM agents requires choosing between teacher-generated demonstrations and student-generated experience, corresponding broadly to off-policy and on-policy distillation. While both have proven effective, we argue that this distinction is inherently hierarchical: the policy that induces an interaction history need not be the same as the policy that generates the token prefix of the current action. We introduce a hierarchical agent distillation framework that formalizes these two levels of on-policiness and enables explicit control over their pairing, mixture proportions, and training schedule. We study this problem through self-distillation, where a frozen teacher has access to task guidance unavailable to the student, and conduct a three-stage controlled study across three models and four interactive environments. We find that student-induced histories paired with teacher-generated action prefixes generally provide the strongest standalone training signal, whereas following the student at both levels generally performs worst. Nevertheless, fully on-policy data becomes beneficial when incorporated into a mixture with other configurations. Moreover, mixtures with identical teacher/student proportions at each level can yield substantially different performance, showing that how the two levels are paired matters beyond the overall amount of on-policy data. Finally, front-loading fully teacher-generated contexts and delaying fully on-policy contexts improves over a static mixture. These findings provide practical principles for deciding where, how much, and when agent distillation should follow the student.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Modern Large Language Models (LLMs) remain vulnerable to carefully orchestrated multi-turn interactions that progressively bypass safety mechanisms. However, many existing red-teaming approaches rely on heuristics or impose strong assumptions that limit their applicability in fully black box settings. We address this gap by introducing $M^3$, an automated red-teaming framework that formalizes multi-turn jailbreaking as a finite-horizon Markov Decision Process (MDP). In $M^3$, an attacker LLM interacts with a target LLM while operating over a compact internal state produced by a state LLM. Across two benchmarks and six target models, $M^3$ consistently outperforms state-of-the-art multi-turn red-teaming methods in Attack Success Rates (ASR), achieving improvements of up to 53% in ASR@5. Even with lightweight attacker, $M^3$ attains gains up to 26% in ASR@1 and 34% in ASR@5, compared to baselines that use higher capacity attackers. Beyond improved effectiveness, $M^3$ exhibits steeper risk escalation over conversational turns and achieves more consistent performance across risk categories (up to 4.82 improvement in Gini index) without relying on predefined risk taxonomy, providing a scalable and principled framework for multi-turn red-teaming and safety evaluation.

Abstract (英文): Large language model (LLM) agents increasingly participate in negotiations on behalf of individuals and organizations, yet they remain vulnerable to manipulation, particularly in repeated multi-party interactions. Classical multi-agent systems address this challenge through explicit trust and reputation models, whereas existing work on LLM negotiation largely treats trust as an emergent phenomenon. We investigate whether LLM agents can form useful trust estimates from prior interactions and leverage them to improve future negotiation outcomes. We introduce a repeated, multi-party, multi-issue negotiation framework in which recurring agents participate in a sequence of negotiation games while one participant covertly attempts to prevent agreement. After each game, agents assess their counterparts and maintain pairwise trust estimates that carry over to subsequent negotiations. We compare trust-aware and trust-agnostic agents and analyze how explicit trust influences negotiation outcomes. Our results reveal a \emph{trust--agreement gap}: trust-aware agents can identify the adversary within a few games, but accurate trust estimates do not consistently translate into better agreements. Trust increases agreement rates by up to 23 percentage points, but only when agents are explicitly guided to use it in their decisions. Explicit trust can therefore improve repeated LLM negotiations, but its value depends critically on how trust is assessed and incorporated into agents' reasoning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Evaluating conversational agents that act on live, mutable enterprise data is hard: replayed sessions drift as backend state changes, and a benchmark built today can silently go stale as the data it describes moves on. We then use this harness as the fitness function for GEPA-based prompt optimization under a correctness gate that guards against reward hacking. Applied to a production merchant-facing agent, correctness-gated compression shrinks the agent's full prompt surface by 3.5-7.6% without regressing held-out task correctness, consistently across three independent subject-model families, and for one, correctness measurably improves.

Abstract (英文): LLM agents are increasingly deployed in collaborative settings where they pursue separate tasks while sharing resources and operating under review or oversight processes. Such settings create safety risks not only when agents are malicious, but also when they are simply trying to help. Prior work has largely studied adversarial or explicitly collusive behavior; we show that benign agents pursuing separate objectives can spontaneously cooperate in ways that bypass intended workflows. We call this the \emph{helpful agent problem}. We study long-horizon shared-resource environments in which agents sometimes depend on resources owned by their peers and cross-owner changes are normally mediated by a reviewer. When review becomes unavailable, agents begin making changes on one another's behalf despite having no shared reward or explicit incentive to cooperate. Crucially, these actions remain within each agent's local permissions while circumventing the system's intended oversight structure. We introduce \textsc{FavorBench}, a controlled benchmark with measurable downstream side effects, and adapt \textsc{CooperBench} to test the phenomenon in realistic repositories. Across two benchmarks and four models, we find that out-of-workflow favors emerge frequently. Further, in \textsc{FavorBench}, favors from at least three models cause significant harm to parties outside the interaction. These results reveal a distinct multi-agent safety challenge: cooperation itself can create pathways around system-level safeguards.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Intent recognition in human-robot interaction requires agents to resolve ambiguous natural language instructions by inferring the speaker's underlying goals. Research has increasingly focused on two notable paradigms for addressing this task: structured symbolic reasoning and multimodal input enrichment. However, it remains unclear whether enrichment-based methods provide benefit in settings where the ambiguity concerns unstated human intent rather than missing observable scene information. In this paper, we present a comparative and diagnostic study benchmarking FISER - a framework utilizing structured social and embodied reasoning - against CEMIR (Context-Enriched Multimodal Intent Recognition), a method that performs multimodal enrichment by synthesizing narratives and photorealistic images from textual prompts. By evaluating both approaches across four Large Language Models (LLMs), ranging from Qwen2.5-Omni-3B to GPT-5, on the HandMeThat v2 benchmark, we analyze their capability to resolve pragmatic ambiguities. Our statistical analysis, employing multiple-comparison correction, reveals no statistically significant difference in performance between FISER and CEMIR across any model or difficulty level. This pattern holds across all sixteen model-by-difficulty comparisons, as well as in a targeted ablation isolating CEMIR's narrative and visual components. Instead, performance is primarily associated with backbone capability and the inherent difficulty of the ambiguity. These findings suggest a boundary condition for text-derived multimodal enrichment: when the relevant task constraints are already available in the textual instruction, synthetic multimodal augmentation may not provide measurable benefit over structured reasoning on the same text.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Task instructions from users for agents are inherently underspecified, especially regarding safety boundaries--creating behavioral risks. To study these risks, we introduce BARC-Bench, a collection of 110 tasks across nine simulated workspace environments where benign requests leave openings for unsafe actions. In this benchmark, we evaluate agent behavior against safety boundaries established by workspace records, implicit environmental context, and unstated social norms. For example, an agent asked to schedule off-boarding discussions must arrange the meeting without prematurely revealing an employee's resignation through a shared calendar invitation. Evaluating four frontier model configurations across five attempts per task reveals that models are not consistently safe: the share of tasks completed safely on all five attempts ranges from 9.1% to 72.7%, with even the strongest configuration producing a safety violation on 27.3% of tasks. These results highlight an asymmetric operational risk: while models frequently complete complex workflows, their inability to consistently respect unstated boundaries presents a substantial barrier to safe autonomous delegation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Conventional neural-network training typically follows a predefined optimization recipe, motivating a LLM agent that monitors training dynamics and adjusts optimizer settings as training progresses, but a fixed agent policy does not accumulate experience across runs. We introduce RSI-Opt, a plug-and-play Recursive Self-Improvement Optimizer Agents which evolves the knowledge that guides this process: a prompt, a playbook of procedures, a library of tested optimizer code, and a decision cadence. A meta-agent rewrites this policy using evidence from earlier runs, and an archive retains evaluated policies for further evolution. Across 5 generations on three training tasks, the meta-agents develop reusable procedures and code and conduct their own CPU proxy experiments. RSI-Opt substantially improves optimizer-agent performance, with representative raw-space summary $z$-scores increasing from $+10.48$ to $+14.33$ on the evolution suite with Opus 5.5 and from $-0.36$ to $+3.11$ on held-out tasks with GPT-5.5 through cross-model policy transfer. On the Burgers PDE, it reduces mean relative $L_2$ error by approximately 2.7-fold with Opus 5.5 and 8.8-fold with GPT-6.1 over the fixed-prompt agent baseline, although PDE transfer varies across LLMs and tasks. Controlled comparisons show that the evolved policy combines reusable optimization knowledge with adaptation to each task: initial decisions capture most observed gains on seen tasks, while continued adaptation improves outcomes on unseen tasks. These findings demonstrate how procedures and executable tools accumulated across training runs can improve optimizer-agent performance and support transfer to scientific machine learning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The growing population of orbital debris has intensified interest in active debris removal and on-orbit servicing, for which dual-arm space manipulators offer improved grasping stability and load distribution relative to single-arm systems. However, coordinating two manipulators under inter-arm collision avoidance, orientation alignment, and sensing uncertainty remains challenging. This paper develops a coordinated reinforcement learning framework for cooperative motion planning of a dual-arm space manipulator in a target-reaching task with inter-arm collision-avoidance constraints. A centralized state representation combines joint-space, end-effector, and target-relative information, while a multi-objective reward function promotes target-reaching accuracy, orientation alignment, motion smoothness, synchronized task completion, and collision avoidance. Within this framework, Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) and Soft Actor-Critic (SAC) are compared as representative on-policy and off-policy algorithms, and the learned policies are further evaluated for generalization to previously unseen target configurations. Across five random seeds, SAC substantially outperforms PPO within the considered fixed-base simulation scenarios: it reaches an 80% success threshold in every seed (median 1.6M environment steps) whereas PPO does not reach it within a 4M-step budget, and it attains a higher mean success rate (78.6% versus 7.4%) with lower position and orientation errors and tighter bimanual synchronization. Both algorithms degrade on unseen boundary targets, but SAC retains most of its performance while PPO's collapses. The results also expose a failure mode of PPO: under the two-phase shaped reward, it tends to converge to a reward-loitering local optimum rather than committing to the precision task. This behavior highlights the sensitivity of on-policy learning to reward shaping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AI-for-Science agents are increasingly being used for formulating scientific hypotheses, running autonomous experiments, and interpreting evidence. These agents are constructed by integrating a large language model (LLM) into a domain-specific agent harness, which in turn can improve the LLM's scientific capability and enable the model to orchestrate scientific tools. In this work, we ask whether model safety is preserved when an LLM is integrated in an AI-for-Science agent harness. We evaluate five agent harnesses on 50 harmful chemistry requests. Specifically, we study how the safety behavior changes under direct prompting, two simple adversarial prompting strategies, and an adaptive attack. Across four recent GPT variants, our results show that the agent harness can increase harmful compliance even under direct prompting. More critically, we find that adaptive attacks that are largely unsuccessful against the base LLM suddenly become highly successful after the agent harness is introduced. Our results show that the safety behavior of the base LLM is not durable once a harness is introduced. Thus, as AI-for-Science agents become more capable and autonomous, the community needs to evaluate and align the agent using domain-specific safety benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent \emph{test-time training} (TTT) frameworks have enabled open-weight large language models (LLMs) to outperform inference-only frameworks powered by frontier models on scientific discovery tasks. However, TTT introduces additional computational cost for online weight updates during the discovery process, making computational efficiency an important consideration when scaling to larger models. This motivates us to explore an efficient \emph{test-time adaptation} method for scientific discovery. We introduce \emph{Test-Time Memory Evolution} (TTME), a plug-and-play test-time adaptation method, as an alternative to TTT. TTME continually updates the external memory by distilling the experience to guide subsequent discovery without updating model weights. To have a clean comparison, we evaluate TTME and TTT under the same discovery framework, replacing only the test-time adaptation method. Our evaluation shows that TTME outperforms TTT on all five mathematical tasks, while reducing computational cost by $82.8$-$84.6\%$ in FLOPs and $47.7$-$58.4\%$ in runtime. These results establish TTME as an effective and computationally efficient alternative to TTT in the evaluated discovery settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present HyScale, a long-context training system for learned sparse attention. Learned sparse attention is a promising architecture for million-token contexts, widely adopted in production since DeepSeek-V3.2. Selection, however, grows into the training bottleneck. For each query, it scores every previous token whereas sparse attention reads only $k$; worse, current systems materialize a full score table and repeat selection during recomputation. Our key insight is that the output of selection is not an activation, but rather a set of indices. Building on it, HyScale introduces HyTopK and selection replay. HyTopK finds each query's exact top-$k$ on the fly without a score table, while selection replay reuses forward-pass selection in recomputation. Together with holistic system optimizations tackling end-to-end training challenges, HyScale speeds up GLM-5.1 training on 1M-token sequences by 2.07$\times$ over Megatron-LM on 512 H20 GPUs. It also speeds up the CSA layers of DeepSeek-V4-Flash and the QSA layers of Qwen3.8-Flash-Next by 2.01$\times$ and 1.57$\times$ at 1M tokens on 32 H800 GPUs.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-agent LLM systems aim to improve reasoning through debate, evidence sharing, and mutual correction, but interaction can also introduce failures that final accuracy alone cannot explain. Agents may converge too early, ignore dissent, suppress correct minority answers, or preserve a wrong framing across rounds. We present a corpus-grounded taxonomy of communication failures, built from 3,211 failed multi-round trajectories across medical QA, coding, finance, multi-hop reasoning, ethics, and embodied text tasks. Using this taxonomy, we analyze failure distributions across datasets and system designs, round-level error dynamics, recovery hardness, majority transitions, minority-signal suppression, and self-reported confidence. We find that failures are dominated by recovery/correction and consensus/decision problems rather than isolated first-round mistakes, with many emerging after agents first observe peer responses. Based on these findings, we recommend stronger disagreement handling, evidence verification, minority-signal preservation, and recovery checks before finalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can perceive a traffic scene, reason about it, and propose a driving action within a single model. However, existing MLLM-based drivers reason from scratch at each frame and commit to a single explanation, discarding earlier hypotheses about the scene. When the scene is ambiguous, the agent locks in on the wrong interpretation and acts on it before evidence accumulates. In this paper, we propose DriveMind, an MLLM-based driving framework that keeps several hypotheses about the scene alive at the same time and updates their weights as new frames arrive. Inspired by particle filtering, DriveMind propagates each hypothesis through a VLM, scores it against the current observation, and resamples to maintain diversity. On Bench2Drive, adding belief tracking to the same driving pipeline improves the Driving Score (DS) from 68.50 to 81.37 and the Success Rate (SR) from 41.36% to 54.09%. The largest gains occur on ambiguous scenarios such as unsignalized intersections (+20.0 DS) and oncoming-traffic interactions (+20.5 DS). Beyond the overall metrics, DriveMind also improves fine-grained interactive driving abilities such as merging, emergency braking, and give-way.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Understanding language-agent behaviour requires learning how actions are organised across a trajectory and how this organisation relates to context and outcomes. We propose a general framework that connects data-driven behavioural discovery with analysis and experimental investigation. Categories developed from recorded activity define what is measured. Analyses preserve the behaviours' positions in the process and express their relationships as interpretable hypotheses. Statistical assessment and tests in held-out contexts examine the strength and scope of the evidence. Experiments, including prompt steering, then test how behaviour and outcomes change under deliberately varied conditions. A study of iterative self-reflection illustrates how discovery, dependency learning and prompt comparisons can inform one another. We develop this approach for trajectories of varying length, event order and agent roles, and identify advances in coding, analysis and experimentation needed to support its wider use.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion language models (DLMs) support bidirectional denoising and iterative refinement, but generation remains largely controlled by token-level masking schedules. This leaves draft-level refinement implicit and limits explicit control over when to insert missing content, delete redundant spans, revisit uncertain regions, or stop editing. We introduce Diffusion Editing Language Adapter (DELA), a plug-in framework that enables controllable draft refinement with a frozen DLM. DELA operates on clean, variable-length drafts using four explicit editing actions: Insert, Delete, Remask, and Stop. The controller determines when and where to edit the draft, while the frozen DLM fills masked regions through denoising, separating high-level edit control from token-level generation. A naive single-phase editor would be computationally expensive because arbitrary interior edits can disrupt prefix stability and limit reliable KV-cache reuse. To address this, DELA uses a two-phase decoding procedure: a fast, append-only drafting phase that supports KV-cache reuse, followed by a global refinement phase that enables targeted non-monotonic edits to the full draft. We train the controller using a staged curriculum that begins with learning local edit priors from synthetic recovery tasks and mined actions that improve draft quality. Subsequent stages refine edit selection through rollout-based outcome preferences and alignment with downstream task feedback. Experiments on mathematical reasoning and code generation show that DELA achieves gains of up to 5.5 percentage points over direct diffusion decoding while keeping the backbone frozen. DELA thus provides a modular approach to explicit draft-level control in diffusion language models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fixed source superposition and frequency partitioning constrain how communication content interacts with a probing reference in integrated sensing and communication (ISAC). We learn nonlinear waveform fusion across scales for image transmission and localization of multiple targets in range and angle. Reception uses the information available at each end. Adaptive temporal feature weighting and spectral updates combine image features and a deterministic linearly frequency-modulated reference in a shared complex state, generating a waveform with fixed total energy over the array frame. At the user equipment (UE), the reference and channel state information condition a neural demixer for image reconstruction. At the base station, the realized transmit pulse defines a coherent dictionary for sequential localization with learned local correction, physical refinement, and residual updates. We compare the proposed waveforms with superposition in the time domain and learned spectral linear fusion on 512 independent test images, using a shared fixed UE receiver and a baseline sensing receiver using coherent peak search. Averaged across eleven task weights, the proposed waveforms reduce normalized localization error by 29.0\% and 27.0\%, respectively, while increasing mean peak signal-to-noise ratio by 1.40 and 0.82~dB. With the proposed waveforms fixed, learned local correction reduces mean normalized localization error by 12.2\% relative to the same sequential receiver without learned correction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Scientific agents collaborate with researchers whose expertise and preferences differ, but benchmarks usually hold the collaborator fixed. We introduce PersonaScienceBench: 200 tasks from five benchmarks across four scientific domains. Simulated researchers provide context and feedback according to persona profiles, while task objectives and grading remain fixed. Agents encounter researcher preferences only through conversation. Across eighteen modelconfigurations, task performance, fact recovery, and self-repetition reveal different strengths. Detailed GPT 6 Astra analyses show distinct agent responses across researcher profiles; interaction traces illustrate targeted clar ification and workflow revision. These behaviors can differ even when final scores match. PersonaScienceBench therefore evaluates how agents collaborate with diverse researchers alongside whether they complete the task.
